The other orientation is exactly

    Q_(16+i,j)=t_scale(barH_i+L_i)-barDelta_j
                                      +V_i Qval_j/R^2.         (44)

The height term is nonpositive. Products of opposite signs are
therefore already covered. Consider first a receiver in block 0 or 2
with atomic index j<3. A positive product requires a source in block
0 or 2, whose V_i is at most m. Dividing the mixed term by the
receiver's s_b E_j, and using l_j/E_j<=rho, gives at most

    Cpair_2 m sigma r_0 Mout_1 rho/R^4
        < Cpair_2/3 < 3/4,                    receiver block 0,
    m sigma r_0 Mout_2 rho/R^4
        <= m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4,   receiver block 2.

These are smaller than rho, so barDelta_j>=rho s_b E_j suffices.
Their atomic endpoint receivers have Qval=barDelta=0 and leave
only the nonpositive height term.

For receivers in blocks 1,3, a positive product requires a source
in blocks 1,3. Let s_source=k,nk and a_source=1,Cpair_1 in these two
source blocks. Its height satisfies

    barH_i+L_i<=-(1-c_lift)chi s_source Patom_i.

Let s_receiver=k,nk and theta_receiver=Cpair_2,1 in receiver blocks
1,3, with r=r_1,r_2 and M_r=Mout_1,Mout_2 respectively. The height term
plus mixed term in (44) is at most

    chi Patom_i[-t_scale(1-c_lift)s_source
           +a_source q_axis s_receiver theta_receiver M_r/R^2]
      +a_source q_axis Patom_i s_receiver theta_receiver
                                      (-chi b_j+r l_j)/R^2.    (45)

The coefficient in the first line is nonpositive. Indeed
a_source/s_source<=1/k, since Cpair_1/n<1. After division by s_source,
the positive term is at most Cpair_1 Cpair_2 for receiver block 1, and
n Cpair_2 for receiver block 3. Both are at most n Cpair_2 because Cpair_1<n.
On the other hand,

    t_scale(1-c_lift)=n v_scale(1-c_lift)>n Cpair_2

by (32). The -chi b_j term in the second line is also nonpositive.
For atomic receiver j<3, its remaining term divided by s_receiver E_j
is at most

    q_axis m r_2 rho/R^2
       =m sigma r_0 r_2 D rho/R^4<3/4<rho.

The receiving energy pays for it. At atomic index j=3 both b_j and
l_j vanish, so the first-line estimate proves the row directly.
This exhausts all 256 reverse cross rows, including every outgoing
terminal row.

The left child contributes 240 ordered within-child rows by sections
2-8. The lifted right child contributes 240 by section 14 and scaling.
The two cross orientations contribute 512 by sections 16-17. The 64
optimum incidences hold by (38) and (36): all physical heights are
nonpositive and all energies are nonnegative. These are precisely
1056 distinct ordered rows on {star,0,...,31}. No original gradient,
queried pair, final sample, or range condition has been removed.

## 18. One global function, its minimizers, and exact normalization

Define ynew_i=Xnew_i-Gnew_i and
Deltanew_i=Fnew_i-||Gnew_i||^2/2. On the same R^9 put

    Hnew(v)=||v||^2/2
               +max(0,max_(0<=i<=31)(<ynew_i,v>-Deltanew_i)),
    F_31(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-Hnew(v)).                    (46)

All original rows have just been proved. Applying the constructive
interpolation proof of accepted fact `1252b8823bb5a290` proves that
F_31 is finite, convex, everywhere differentiable, and 1-smooth,
with F_31(Xnew_i)=Fnew_i, gradient Gnew_i, and minimum value zero.
This is one static function chosen before the run, not a sequence
of changed functions.

If chi>0, the zero energies occur at left indices 3,7,11,15 and
right indices 19,27. Their residuals are, respectively,

    0, E_2 E_top t, 0, E_2 B_top t, 0, 0.

Every other energy is positive: in particular the newly lifted right
endpoint energies at 23 and 31 are t_scale eps_lift and
t_scale n eps_lift. Thus

    argmin F_31=conv{0,E_2 E_top t,E_2 B_top t}.      (47)

To check this directly, for any fixed v and sufficiently small
epsilon>0, the maximum of affine terms in Hnew(epsilon v) retains
only the zero-energy slopes. Its linear term is the support function
of the hull in (47). Points in that hull have conjugate value zero.
For a point outside, its difference from the closest point of the
hull is a strict separating direction; sufficiently small epsilon
in that direction gives a positive conjugate value. This proves
both inclusions. At chi=0 all translations and increments vanish,
and the same formula gives the singleton {0}.

Section 9 proved that <Z_0,z><=0 for every
z in conv{0,E_top t,B_top t}. The isometry E_2 preserves this relation,
and ||Xnew_0||=R. Therefore for every minimizer z in (47),

    ||Xnew_0-z||^2=R^2+||z||^2-2<Xnew_0,z>>=R^2.

Zero attains equality. The TRUE initial distance is exactly R. Neither
internal residual nor the new endpoint energy has been hidden in a
surrogate distance to a more remote minimizer.

Let N_3=||Gnew_31||^2=t_scale N_2. With
tau=chi D/N and nu_res=chi^2 R^2/N from section 10, the exact terminal
quantities are

    Deltanew_31=c_lift tau N_3,
    2F_31(Xnew_31)=N_3(1+2c_lift tau),
    <Gnew_31,ynew_31>/N_3=tau,
    ||ynew_31||^2/N_3=nu_res.                       (48)

The actual normalized payoff and capacity state are consequently

    V_31=t_scale n k N(1+tau/50)/R^2,
    kappa_31=(lambda^3 A)^2 V_31
        =Fshape_2 kappa_15^2(1+tau/50)>1+1/600.     (49)

Indeed tau<1/3000, r_2>14, and nu_res>=0 give
Fshape_2>1-1/21000. Section 10 gives kappa_15>1+1/1000.
It therefore suffices that
1/500-1/21000-1/10500000>1/600; subtracting 1/600 leaves
1/3500-1/10500000>0.

The exact comparison to the ORIGINAL benchmark is

    W_31(h_31)>=V_31,
    V_31/U_31^ZJ = B_31 kappa_31/(lambda^6 A^2),     (50)

where B_31 is defined in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`. Capacity squared
has not replaced B_31. Rescaling F_31(Rx)/R^2 gives true distance one
and the same normalized payoff; multiplication by any smoothness
constant L_smooth>0 gives the corresponding steps h_i/L_smooth.
This is the original doubled-gap W=2R convention.

## 19. Why copied values fail and what the new boundary actually costs

The extra values are essential for this concrete join when chi>0.
If L_i in (28) were zero, the actual queried row with source 15 and
receiver 23 would be

    Q_(15,23)=chi n k rho(Lcoef_2-z)>0.              (51)

It follows from (39): source 15 is block 3's endpoint, and right
local 7 is block 1's endpoint. To see the strict sign uniformly,
sigma kappa_3>1+1/3000 by section 10, while
delta r_0D/R^2<1/50000 by (11). Equation (35) implies Lcoef_2/z>1.
The new receiver increment subtracts t_scale eps_lift from this
row. Inequalities (34) and (32) show that this more than pays its
positive part. The reverse row is separately checked in section 17;
there is no invalid two-row or three-row sign cancellation.

Thus the fixed-value continuation fails, while this value-reselected
point of the full affine model succeeds at the level of the joint
draft. This is not a separator of the released affine family and
not a claim that it was necessary to change the queried geometry.

The full new boundary, not just its scalar payoff, is

    ynew_i=E_2 barY_i, Deltanew_i=barDelta_i,
    Hnewheight_i=barH_i,                             0<=i<=15,
    ynew_(16+j)=B_2 barY_j,
    Deltanew_(16+j)=t_scale(barDelta_j+L_j),
    Hnewheight_(16+j)=t_scale(barH_j+L_j),            0<=j<=15.
                                                               (52)

Here Hnewheight denotes Fnew-<Gnew,Xnew>+||Gnew||^2/2 and must not
be confused with the conjugate function Hnew in (46). In particular
the endpoint height is -(1-c_lift)tau N_3, its energy is
c_lift tau N_3, and the earlier two paid deficits remain in the left
child and in the appropriately scaled right copy. All these data
must be retained or re-priced by any further parent.

For orientation only, at the next physical bridge
r_3=lambda^2 A, the outgoing norm would be

    ||ynew_31-r_3 Gnew_31||^2
        =N_3(r_3^2-2r_3 tau+nu_res).

If another origin-aligned join with copied endpoint value were legal,
its FORMAL objective-state law would be

    kappa_63,formal
       =(1-2tau/r_3+nu_res/r_3^2)
                           kappa_31^2/(1+tau/50).   (53)

The denominator is required: the actual31 gap is larger than its
squared terminal-gradient norm. Dropping that factor would confuse
the new positive energy with a free norm gain. Formula (53) is not
a feasibility assertion, and no actual63 construction or separator
is claimed here. This round has tested the assigned NEXT nonempty
extension, namely actual15 to actual31.

## 20. Scope, audit, and bounded handoff

This manuscript includes every new assertion it uses: the atom, both
internal joins, the actual15 value deficits, the new atom-wise lifts,
both orientations of all31 cross rows, one explicit static function,
its full minimizer set, true radius, and the resulting boundary state.
The two accepted predecessors used are `1252b8823bb5a290` for
interpolation/realization and `b98bb807d514e80d` for benchmark notation.
No unverified shared finding is a mathematical predecessor.

The counterexample screen explicitly rejects the copied-value
shortcut through (51). It also keeps sigma=1: then chi=eps_lift=0,
the problematic row becomes equality, and every proof above still

fully released parent model were used; no impossibility statement

or a fixed final gradient.

Retrieval was conditioned on the mature affine-interpolation program.
Matlas returned the smooth interpolation statement in arXiv:1910.01845,
Theorem 5, plus two unrelated results. No new external theorem was
imported. The exact local Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur paper,

premise remains the independently accepted proof in `1252b8823bb5a290`.
Reference: https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.05666.

All new mathematics remains UNVERIFIED. This is a complete finite
construction candidate, not a verifier verdict or a new fact. Current
TASK.md and master guidance `8211d6128deabdf3`, reconfirmed by
`147c08e9addc6a0b`, allocate zero numerical,

# Discrete amplitude capture and reduction to the sole root reversal

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No verifier submission; no new accepted fact.**

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926, worker max3, invocation84 after ended83,
completed ceiling84 / attempted85, controller cap1. Operative guidance
63c13a78e4758ad6; plan00bca57bc8f73bdb. This is one joint mathematical
draft. Prior reports a0925e826bbc6304 and d34bc8275bb55379 supplied awareness;
none of their unverified claims is used without a derivation below.

The requested gradient constant3/2 is **neither proved nor refuted**.
There is a new all-depth discrete-amplitude bound, a resulting all-start
objective theorem when kappa*S_m>=12, and a reduction of the remaining
inner-reversing cases to the original root bridge alone. This does not
close the whole odd family or the original minimax problem.

One substantive mechanism was used: exact interval/capacity control of
the prescribed fixed odd function. Its two direct attempts are the
successful discrete amplitude invariant and a failed widening of that
same invariant. The latter has a legal complete-child counterexample,
not a full-H_m counterexample. No paid-variable second mechanism is
claimed or opened, and no third direction is introduced.

All displayed recursions are proved symbolically, not executed. Resource
use: zero mathematical programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory replay,
solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls/fact_submit, installations,
consults, or subagents. File reads/writes and hashes are administrative.

## 1. Exact problem data and conclusions

Fix an integer m>=1536. At every child retain the same parameters

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, h_j^lev=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,h_j^lev,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j,
    S=S_m, D=1+2S, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a.

The chronological physical entries of H_m are h_i, 0<=i<n. The last
physical step is ell. The symbol d_m does not denote an additional step.
All symbols in this report have the local meanings specified by TASK.md
and this section. In particular G, H_j, M_j, and P_j are not the unrelated
mass-join, conjugate-jet, mass, or prefix symbols found in older project
glossary entries.

Fix finite B>=kappa and 0<kappa<=1. Put

    v=1-kappa, u=v+B,
    G(r)=min(B,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(x)=sign(x)*G(|x|), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F(r)=f(r) for r>=0.

For the ONE fixed function, Psi_j(s) is the complete H_j output from s,
and M_j(R)=max_(|s|<=R)|Psi_j(s)|. The true starting radius of an actual
trajectory is R=|s|. The objective payoff is 2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2. A child
image radius never replaces that denominator.

The new joint conclusions are:

1. For every j>=1 and every integer q>=0,

       G(M_j(d_j*c^(-q)*B)) <= a*c^(-q)*B.        (1.1)

   All q use the original kappa, B, and knot radius1. This is a discrete
   amplitude statement, not the known-false assertion for every w.

2. For every j>=1 and every R>0,

       G(M_j(R)) <= beta*R/d_j.                  (1.2)

   Every complete-child history containing a strict sign crossing has
   terminal gradient at most aB, hence has terminal radius strictly below u.

3. For every allowed kappa,B and every nonzero actual start, if

       kappa*S >=12,

   then

       2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <=1/D.                   (1.3)

   Crossing histories have the strict quantified margin

       D*2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <58089/58800<1.        (1.4)

4. Jointly rederived here, the complementary no-inner-reversal theorem is

       kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1
       => 2f(Psi_m(s))/s^2 <=[1+21/(4m)]/D       (1.5)

   for every start and every B>=kappa.

5. Every case not covered by (1.3) or (1.5) satisfies

       5<kappa*S<12,
       kappa*(1+d_(m-2))<1<kappa*(1+d_(m-1)).   (1.6)

   Thus J=min{j:kappa*(1+d_j)>1} equals m-1. The sole possible inner
   reversing level is the ORIGINAL ROOT. Each of its two complete H_(m-1)
   children has an absorbing core. Exterior sign crossings can still occur
   inside either child; (1.6) does not remove them.

6. If the requested3/2 gradient estimate holds on all remaining crossing
   trajectories, then their payoff is strictly below1/D, with conditional
   coefficient1809/2000. Together with (1.3),(1.5), this would give the
   whole-family bound [1+21/(4m)]/D. Section8 proves this implication without
   assuming its missing gradient hypothesis.

Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 concerns the FULL smooth-convex class and a
conditional benchmark separation. Even a completed theorem for the present
odd scalar family would not supply that full-class hypothesis. No original
target completion or minimax conclusion is claimed.

## 2. Function legality, exact areas, and the pre-cap conversion

The true radial energy is

    2F(r)=kappa*r^2,                         0<=r<=1,
    2F(r)=(r-v)^2+kappa*v,                   1<=r<=u,
    2F(r)=2B*r-C_tail,                       r>=u,
    C_tail=B^2+v*(2B-kappa).                       (2.1)

At r=1 the adjacent formulas both give kappa. At r=u they both give
B^2+kappa*v. Their first derivatives also match. The middle interval is
empty when B=kappa; v=0 when kappa=1. The formulas include both ties.

The derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing. Its piecewise slopes
are kappa,1,0, so partitioning any interval at the knots shows that it
is1-Lipschitz. Consequently f is finite, differentiable, convex, and
globally1-smooth. Since G(r)>0 for r>0, its unique minimizer is0 and
f(0)=0. The actual initial distance is |s|.

For any x<y let Delta=g(y)-g(x). Then0<=Delta<=y-x. On[x,y],

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)).

Integrating proves both directed smooth-convex interpolation inequalities.
Thus all actual physical, terminal, and optimum samples satisfy every
ordered row

    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.

The Gram matrix is positive semidefinite because its quadratic forms are
squares of real linear combinations of actual scalar vectors. Repeated
visits carry the same values and derivatives. Every physical step and the
original terminal are present.

At r with p=G(r), monotonicity and smoothness give G(t)<=min(t,p) on[0,r].
Since p<=r, integration gives

    2F(r)<=2r*p-p^2.                              (2.2)

For r<=u, the stronger conversion needed here is

    2F(r)<=G(r)^2/kappa.                          (2.3)

In the core this is equality. In the middle, p=r-v>=kappa and

    p^2/kappa-(p^2+kappa*v)
       =v*(p^2-kappa^2)/kappa>=0.

This calculation includes r=1,u, B=kappa, and v=0. For r>u, (2.3) is
not asserted: the energy continues growing while the gradient is capped.
This is why the post-crossing pre-cap conclusion is essential.

For any advertised L>0, use L*f with steps h_i/L. The points and normalized
payoff remain the same. A smaller actual core curvature never rescales
the physical word or changes the project normalization mathcal W=2*mathcal R.

## 3. Same-m masses and elementary margins

Throughout this report,

    0<epsilon<1/1000, beta>999/1000,
    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    2/5<a<1/2, beta>c-2,
    c*a=beta<1.                                  (3.1)

The c bounds follow from7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12 and
(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7. The bound a>2/5 follows from
beta>99/100 and c<17/7; the remaining bounds follow by substitution.

Set eta=a/(c-2). Since lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda,
c(c-2)>1>beta, so0<eta<1. The literal word recursion gives

    A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                      (3.2)

Substitution verifies the formula at j=0 and the recursion. Hence

    A_j>d_j/(c-2),
    A_j<c^j for j>=1,
    (c-2)d_j<A_j,
    D<2c^m=2d_m/a.                               (3.3)

For L_j=S_j/d_j and j>=1,

    L_j>7/5.                                     (3.4)

Indeed L_1=ell/beta>7/5. If L_j>7/5, then
L_(j+1)=(2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c>(19/5)/(17/7)>7/5.

We use the following generous finite-horizon margins:

    S>1000, d_(m-1)>9, d_(m-1)>1/epsilon,
    d_m>2/a, d_m^2>D, D/S<201/100.               (3.5)

Since a>2/5 and c>2, d_(m-1)>2^m/5. The inequality2^m>20m for m>=8
follows from2^8>20*8 and induction by doubling. This proves
d_(m-1)>4m=1/epsilon. The other size claims follow for m>=1536 from
the same bound, S>=1+d_(m-1), a>2/5, and (3.3). In particular
D/S=2+1/S<201/100.

At any level-k bridge in a complete H_j, let P be its actual preceding
mass inside that word. Then P>=S_k, since its immediate left H_k is
present. After that bridge the actual suffix consists of

    H_k, h_(l_1)^lev,H_(l_1), ..., h_(l_t)^lev,H_(l_t),
    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                          (3.6)

Ascend the binary word tree: retain precisely the ancestors entered
through their left child; each contributes its bridge and its full right
child. Ancestors entered through a right child contribute nothing.
Equation(3.6) preserves all skipped levels and the actual final leaf.

## 4. Exact interval map and the original first-crossing price

For a physical h>=1 write d=h-1. The exact image radius of[-R,R] is

    E_h(R)=max{
       |1-h*kappa|*min(R,1),
       [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,
       [R-h*B]_+
    },                                             (4.1)

where[z]_+=max(z,0). On the positive core the signed output is
(1-h*kappa)r; on the middle it is -d*r+h*v; on the tail it is r-hB.
Each piece is affine, so the maximum absolute value occurs at an endpoint
or a knot. When R excludes a piece, its formal term in(4.1) is dominated
by an included endpoint. This proves(4.1), including both knot ties.

Each signed update is continuous, odd, and fixes0. Its image of a symmetric
interval is the entire symmetric interval of radius E_h(R), by the
intermediate value property. Consequently

    M_(j+1)=M_j composed with E_(1+d_j) composed with M_j. (4.2)

These compositions describe compatible actual starts, rather than
independently selected endpoint data. The image maps are nondecreasing.

For a strict sign crossing at source radius y and gradient p=G(y),

    landing=(1+d)*p-y<=d*p,                       (4.3)

because p<=y. If a pre-cap source has a positive same-side output, then
kappa*h<1 and that output lies in the core. In the core it is
(1-h*kappa)y; in the middle it is

    1-h*kappa-d*(y-1)<=1-h*kappa<1.              (4.4)

In both cases it is at most(1-h*kappa)y and at most1-h*kappa.
If the source is the cap knot, either adjacent formula gives the same
actual value; we may classify it as saturated.

Whenever every step of a word satisfies kappa*h<=1, a trajectory begun
in the core remains there with nonnegative factors1-kappa*h. For a
complete H_j in this situation define

    P_j(kappa)=product_(h in H_j)(1-kappa*h).

Then0<=P_j<=exp(-kappa*S_j), because1-z<=exp(-z) for z>=0.
This is an exact product over every physical leaf, followed by a bound;
no absent child is inserted.

Suppose a complete H_j is in that no-inner-reversal situation and an
actual trajectory from R contains a strict crossing. Its first crossing
has an exterior source y>1: a previous core entry would be absorbing.
Let its level be k, its source gradient p, and its actual prefix mass P.
Then y>=p+v and strict crossing implies p>v/d_k. Before this first
crossing, radii decrease and all preceding gradients are at least p.
Thus, with Q=(c-1)/(c-2),

    R>=y+Pp>=v+(1+P)p
      >v*(1+(1+P)/d_k)
      >=v*(1+A_k/d_k)>vQ.                       (4.5)

Here v>0: a nonempty no-inner-reversal word has kappa<1. The price
uses the ORIGINAL input to the complete word and its actual spent mass.
It is independent of where a later core entry occurs.

## 5. Joint discrete-amplitude induction and crossing capture

We prove (1.1) for all q>=0, together with

    any crossing in complete H_j
       => terminal gradient <=aB.               (5.1)

The crossing consequence for H_j uses only plateaus at proper-child
levels. Indeed, after a level-k crossing (4.3) gives landing<=d_kB.
For k>=1 the immediately following full H_k gives gradient<=aB by
its q=0 plateau. For k=0 there is no following H_0 step and the landing
itself has gradient<=aB.

At a retained later ancestor l in(3.6), the incoming gradient is<=aB<B,
so the input is pre-cap. If its bridge crosses, its landing is<=d_l*aB
<=d_lB; the following full H_l gives gradient<=aB. If it does not
cross, (4.4) sends it into the core without increasing gradient, and
kappa*(1+d_l)<1 makes all steps of the following H_l nonreversing in
the core. A zero hit stays zero. Thus every actual retained ancestor is
accounted for, while skipped ancestors supply no factor. This proves
(5.1) once the proper-child q=0 plateaus are available.

For the base j=1, put w=c^(-q)B and input bound R=d_1w=beta*w.
There is no positive tail stay since R<=B and ell>1. Every crossing
has landing<=a*G(y)<=a*R=a*beta*w. Every interior same-side stay has
gradient at most kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+*min(R,1).
The elementary quadratic maximum gives

    kappa*[1-ell*kappa]_+<=1/(4ell)<a,

where4aell>4*(2/5)*(7/5)>1. Therefore this case also gives gradient
<=aR<a*w. The absolute core-crossing multiplier causes no additional
case because it was already covered by(4.3). This proves(1.1) at j=1
for every q. The preceding suffix argument gives(5.1) at j=1.

Assume the grid bounds and crossing capture through j>=1. Start H_(j+1)
at an arbitrary actual radius R<=d_(j+1)c^(-q)B=c*d_j*w, w=c^(-q)B.
Let y be the first-child output radius. We split the original bridge
according to its actual source and destination.

### 5.1 Bridge crossing

If q=0, G(y)<=B=w. If q>=1, the first-child input bound equals
d_j*c^(-(q-1))*B, so its grid bound gives

    G(y)<=a*c^(-(q-1))*B=beta*w<=w.

The bridge landing is consequently<=d_j*w. The complete second child
and its q-th grid bound give terminal gradient<=a*w.

### 5.2 Saturated same-side bridge

The first child cannot have contained a crossing, by(5.1), since its
terminal gradient would then be<=aB<B. Its terminal radius y is>=u,
and the noncrossing first child has decreasing radii, so every one of
its queries is saturated. Hence y=R-S_jB exactly. The bridge staying
radius z satisfies

    z=R-(S_j+1+d_j)B
      <=c*d_j*w-(A_j+d_j)B<=d_j*w.

For the last inequality, use w<=B and(c-2)d_j<A_j. The second-child
grid again gives terminal gradient<=a*w. This reasoning includes y=u.
An upper bound involving max(u,R-S_jB) alone would not justify this case;
the absence of a first-child crossing is essential.

### 5.3 Interior same-side bridge, no crossing in the first child

If z=0 the conclusion holds. Otherwise kappa*(1+d_j)<1, and the full
second child stays in the core. Set t=kappa*d_j and L=L_j=S_j/d_j.
Monotonicity along the noncrossing first child gives

    R>=y+S_j*G(y)>=(1+kappa*S_j)*y.

The last inequality uses that this particular source y is pre-cap,
so G(y)>=kappa*y; it makes no assertion G(r)>=kappa*r on the tail.
By(4.4) and the actual second-child product,

    p_out<=kappa*exp(-L*t)*(1-kappa-t)*R/(1+L*t)
         <=c*t*(1-t)*exp(-L*t)/(1+L*t)*w
         <=c*t*(1-t)/(1+L*t)^2*w
         <=c/[4*(1+L)]*w<a*w.                   (5.2)

The exponential step uses exp(z)>=1+z for z>=0. The rational maximum
follows from the exact identity

    (1+L*t)^2-4*(1+L)*t*(1-t)=(1-(L+2)*t)^2.

Finally c/[4(1+L)]<(17/7)/(4*(12/5))=85/336<2/5<a.

### 5.4 Interior same-side bridge, a crossing in the first child

Again z>0 forces kappa*(1+d_j)<1. Thus every step of that first H_j
is nonreversing in the core, and its original first crossing satisfies
the price(4.5): R>vQ. The bridge output is at most1-kappa*(1+d_j)=v-t.
The complete right child has the same actual nonnegative core product,
so

    p_out<=kappa*(v-t)*exp(-L*t).

Since R<=c*d_j*w and R>vQ, we have w>vQ/(c*d_j). Therefore

    p_out/w < c*t*(v-t)*exp(-L*t)/(vQ)
            <=c*v/(4Q)<=c/(4Q)<a.              (5.3)

Here0<t<v, and t(v-t)<=v^2/4. Since c-2<3/7, Q>10/3; hence
c/(4Q)<(17/7)/(40/3)=51/280<2/5<a. This charges the first exterior
crossing once against the original input. No amplitude rescaling changes
the function, and no O(1/m) error is spent per level.

The four cases are exhaustive. They prove all parent grid bounds. The
suffix argument at the start of this section, using only proper-child
plateaus, proves parent crossing capture. The joint induction is complete.

## 6. The global gradient estimate and full Huber reference

For0<R<=d_jB choose an integer q>=0 such that

    d_j*c^(-(q+1))*B<R<=d_j*c^(-q)*B.

At a boundary choose the next interval, so the strict lower inequality
always holds. By monotonicity and(1.1),

    G(M_j(R))<=a*c^(-q)*B< a*c*R/d_j=beta*R/d_j. (6.1)

For R>d_jB, consider any actual start |s|<=R. If its trajectory crosses,
(5.1) gives terminal gradient<=aB<beta*R/d_j. If it does not cross,
let r,p be its terminal radius and gradient. Every queried gradient is
at least p, giving |s|>=r+S_jp>=A_jp. By(3.3) and beta>c-2,

    p<=R/A_j<beta*R/d_j.                         (6.2)

Zero hits cause no difficulty. Taking the maximum proves(1.2). The
strict versions are not needed in its stated weak inequality.

The same noncrossing argument together with(2.2) yields, for an arbitrary
noncrossing segment of mass T and initial radius R,

    2F(r)<=2R*p-(1+2T)*p^2<=R^2/(1+2T).          (6.3)

In particular every full-word no-crossing history has payoff<=1/D.

We need the unit-curvature Huber reference only to rederive(1.5). Let
J_B have derivative sign(x)*min(|x|,B). It is the present family member
kappa=1 with cap B>=1; arbitrary B>0 follows by the radius scaling
J_B(x)=B^2*J_1(x/B), which leaves the physical word unchanged.
For B=1, any full-word crossing history has terminal radius<=a<1 by
(5.1), so2J_1 at that point equals the squared terminal gradient.
If R<=d_m, (6.1) bounds its payoff by beta^2/d_m^2<1/D. If R>d_m,
capture bounds its payoff by a^2/R^2<a^2/d_m^2<1/D. The inequalities
d_m^2>D and a,beta<1 were proved in(3.5). Noncrossing histories use
(6.3). Scaling therefore gives, for every B>0 and every actual start,

    2J_B(Psi_m^Huber(s))/s^2<=1/D.              (6.4)

The notation Psi_m^Huber denotes the actual H_m trajectory on J_B.
This reference theorem was derived jointly from(1.1), not imported from
an earlier unverified report.

## 7. All starts above the curvature-mass threshold

Suppose kappa*S>=12. For a noncrossing trajectory use(6.3).
For a crossing trajectory, (5.1) gives terminal gradient p<=aB<B.
Since G reaches B exactly at u and is strictly increasing before u,
the actual terminal radius is strictly below u. Apply(2.3) and(1.2)
at the TRUE original radius R:

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2 <= D*beta^2/(kappa*d_m^2)
      =(D/S)*(beta*S/d_m)^2/(kappa*S).

From A_m<c^m=d_m/a we obtain beta*S/d_m<c. With(3.1),(3.5),

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2
      < (201/100)*(17/7)^2/12
      =58089/58800<1.                            (7.1)

This proves(1.3)-(1.4) for every start, every cap, and every crossing
history, including exterior first-child output and arbitrary later core
entries. Neither the number of crossings nor the number of retained
ancestor levels occurs in the constant.

## 8. Exact implication of the still-missing3/2 estimate

Assume kappa*(1+d_(m-1))>1 and put d=d_(m-1). Then

    kappa*S > S/(1+d)
       > [c*d/(c-2)-1]/(1+d)>5.                (8.1)

For the last step, c/(c-2)>17/3, so
(c/(c-2)-5)*d>(2/3)*9=6 by d>9. This is equivalent to the last
strict comparison in(8.1).

Now suppose, as an EXPLICIT ADDITIONAL HYPOTHESIS on a crossing history,

    p_n<=(3/2)*R/S.                              (8.2)

The already jointly proved capture gives r_n<u, so(2.3) yields

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2
       <=(D/S)*(9/4)/(kappa*S)
       <(201/100)*(9/4)/5=1809/2000<1.           (8.3)

No-crossing histories again use(6.3). Thus(8.2), even only on the
remaining reversing crossing histories, would close that region at1/D.
Section9 handles the no-inner-reversal region independently. The exact
conditional whole-family conclusion would be[1+21/(4m)]/D<17/(16D).
The hypothesis(8.2) has not been established by(1.2).

## 9. Joint no-inner-reversal objective bound

Suppose kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1. Every physical step then preserves and
contracts the core. No-crossing histories use(6.3). For a crossing history
that ever enters the core, (4.5), applied to the ORIGINAL full word,
gives R>vQ. The terminal remains core, hence2F(r_n)<=kappa and

    2F(r_n)/R^2<kappa/(v^2*Q^2).                (9.1)

This includes entry at the final leaf, with no post-entry factor assumed.

If a crossing history never enters the core, every query and the terminal
have radius>1. A middle same-side update would land below1 by(4.4), so
each update is either a crossing or a saturated same-side stay. For a
crossing, its radius is<=d*min(r,B); a saturated stay equals r-hB.
Both are bounded by the Huber interval map

    V_(h,B)(r)=max(d*min(r,B),r-hB).

Its monotone composition bounds the actual terminal radius by the
complete Huber interval radius from the same R. Since G<=min(r,B),
we have F<=J_B pointwise. Equation(6.4) gives payoff<=1/D.
Thus the crossing/entry case(9.1) is the only further estimate needed.

Set t_c=c*(c-2), d=d_(m-1). The function z/(1-z)^2 increases on(0,1).
From kappa<=1/(1+d) we get kappa/v^2<=(1+d)/d^2. By D<2c^m,
d=a*c^(m-1), and(c-1)^2=t_c+1,

    D*kappa/(v^2*Q^2)
      < [2*t_c^2/(beta*(t_c+1))]*(1+1/d).       (9.2)

Indeed both sides before the strict D substitution equal the corresponding
product of (1+d)/d^2 and(c-2)^2/(c-1)^2; expanding a=beta/c gives the
displayed right-hand coefficient.

The identity lambda^2=2lambda+1 gives

    t_c=1+2*(lambda+1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2
       <1+8epsilon,

using lambda<5/2 and epsilon<1/1000. Also t_c>1 and d>1/epsilon.
Therefore(9.2) is strictly less than

    (1+8epsilon)^2*(1+epsilon)/(1-epsilon).

The exact difference

    (1-epsilon)*(1+19epsilon)-(1+8epsilon)^2
       =2epsilon-83epsilon^2>0

shows that this last quantity is less than
(1+19epsilon)*(1+epsilon)<=1+21epsilon. Thus(1.5) follows, with the
absolute error constant21/4, independently of kappa,B,start,m.
For m>=1536, 1+21/(4m)<17/16. All branch ties were included above.

## 10. Only the root can still reverse the core

A remaining case must have kappa*S<12 and kappa*(1+d_(m-1))>1.
Section8 gives its lower bound kappa*S>5.
From (3.3), d_m<(c-2)(S+1), and d_(m-2)=d_m/c^2. Moreover

    (c-2)/c^2<3/40.                              (10.1)

To check(10.1), the polynomial3c^2-40c+80 decreases on the present
interval and has value27/49>0 at c=17/7. Dividing by40c^2 gives(10.1).
Consequently

    kappa*(1+d_(m-2))
      <12*(1+d_(m-2))/S
      <12*[1/S+(3/40)*(1+1/S)]
      =9/10+129/(10S)<1,                        (10.2)

because S>1000. Every smaller level is also nonreversing in the core.
Thus J=m-1, as claimed. This is a conclusion about the SAME full word,
not a restart at a new enclosing parameter.

The previous core-output-gate report a0925e826bbc6304 concerns a subset
of this residual domain. It is not invoked to prove(10.2) or any theorem
in this draft. As strategic awareness, intersecting that earlier draft
with the present reduction leaves its exterior INITIAL left-child output
at the original root. The present joint proof alone leaves the full
transition band(1.6) unresolved. In particular, no later positive inner
reversing ancestor is needed to describe the remaining difficulty.

## 11. Why the new grid does not yet give3/2

The coefficient supplied by(1.2) in units R/S is

    K_m=beta*S_m/d_m.

The exact mass formula gives

    K_m=beta/(c-2)
          +c*(1-eta)*(2/c)^m-beta/d_m.           (11.1)

In particular K_m tends to1+sqrt(2) as m tends to infinity. For clarity,
epsilon tends to0, beta tends to1, c tends to lambda, eta tends to1;
the second term tends to0 since2/c<5/6 and1-eta<1, while d_m grows.
The limiting coefficient exceeds3/2. This is the coefficient of an
upper estimate, not an attained lower bound or a counterexample to(8.2).

A concrete natural attempt was to enlarge each grid radius by
T_grid=13/8 while keeping its gradient output unchanged:

    G(M_j(T_grid*d_j*c^(-q)*B))<=a*c^(-q)*B.    (11.2)

At the full depth such a bound, combined with the same cap/noncrossing
split outside its largest radius, would be enough for3/2. Indeed its
interpolated coefficient is beta*S/(T_grid*d_m)<c/T_grid, and
(17/7)/(13/8)=136/91<3/2. Outside R>T_grid*d_mB a crossing has gradient
<=aB<beta*R/(T_grid*d_m), while a no-crossing trajectory has p<=R/A_m;
the latter is already<R/S<(3/2)R/S, so it causes no additional burden.

However(11.2), quantified over ALL child depths as an induction state,
is false. Fix the allowed unit Huber kappa=B=1, set j=1,q=1, and use
the actual start

    R=T_grid*beta/c.

Since R<(13/8)/(12/5)=65/96<1, the sole H_1 update is quadratic and
lands at radius aR<1. Thus

    G(M_1(R))=aR=T_grid*beta*a/c>a/c,            (11.3)

because T_grid*beta>1. Every point and the exact objective are those of
the same fixed legal Huber function. The step is the actual first physical
leaf of the prescribed H_m, and the complete H_1 child is retained.

Equation(11.3) is a decisive obstruction to the uniform widened-child
state(11.2). It is NOT a violation at the full depth j=m, and does not
refute the requested complete-word3/2 target. A corrected state could
separate the actual quadratic product from the exterior capture and
introduce the larger radius only after enough physical contraction, but
no quantified all-depth repair is established here. Missing child factors
cannot be inserted to create that contraction.

## 12. Remaining interface and administrative conclusion

The new argument removes all arbitrary-history cases with kappa*S>=12
from the odd-family objective problem, and leaves only the root transition
band(1.6) in addition to already stated draft-level scoped information.
The exact outstanding coarse-gradient task can therefore be localized to
actual crossing histories in that band, or replaced there by a direct
objective bound with an absolute total constant. Both children have
nonreversing core dynamics but may contain exterior crossings and a final
same-side entry. Their ORIGINAL radius, spent mass, knot areas, and actual
terminal must still be retained.

There is no legal full-H_m violation of the3/2 target in this report.
There is no whole-family absolute-C0 objective theorem: the residual root
band has not been closed. No fact_id was created and no formal correctness
authority was consulted. All new mathematics remains JOINT UNVERIFIED.

The literature check used Matlas for the precise long-step last-gradient
mechanism. Its results were bounded/constant-step statements that do not
apply. A web fallback surfaced original-silver s-composability background;
its stated original-schedule premise does not establish the altered
same-m word here. No external theorem or paper is used in this proof.
No new machinery or function family was opened.

The authorized one-invocation cap is respected: no automatic renewal,
TASK overwrite, worker/service control, outward publication, finalization,
revocation, shutdown, or partial goal completion. Return with this exact
open interface; do not describe the cap stop as mathematical completion.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002987 -->
# Exact terminal hull and one-step minimax continuation

Status: complete candidate derivation, NOT verifier-accepted. The only proof
predecessor is fact `1252b8823bb5a290`. The selected terminal-reduction submission
was already accepted in the preceding round; its one-request allocation is used.
This file contains one self-contained follow-on candidate and no new submission.

## 1. Quantifiers and the complete prefix

Fix an integer n >= 1 and any fixed nonnegative prefix

    h_pre = (h_0,...,h_(n-2)).

The prefix is empty when n=1. Set k=n-1. The last step b_last ranges over
[0,infinity), and the full schedule is (h_pre,b_last). Use the normalized
setting L=1, x_star=g_star=f_star=0 and initial squared distance at most one.
The quantity W_n is exactly the normalized, arbitrary-finite-dimensional
worst-case objective in fact 1252b8823bb5a290.

Let C_pre be the set of pairs C=(G_pre,(f_i)_(0<=i<=k)) satisfying:

* G_pre is the PSD Gram matrix of all formal vectors (x_0,g_0,...,g_k);
* its x_0 diagonal is at most one;
* x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t for 0<=i<=k;
* EVERY ordered inequality Q_ij<=0 is retained on J_pre={star,0,...,k},
  where Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

These conditions are imposed on Gram/value coordinates, so C_pre is a
finite-dimensional convex closed set. It is compact: the interpolation
construction in the cited fact realizes every feasible set of prefix jets
as a legal convex 1-smooth function. With D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t),
one has ||x_i||,||g_i||<=D_i and 0<=f_i<=D_i^2/2. Cauchy--Schwarz bounds
every Gram entry. The set contains the zero data and is nonempty.
This argument allows all zero steps and imposes no strictness condition.

Write Delta_pre for the probability simplex on J_pre. For every C and
alpha in Delta_pre set

    y_j=x_j-g_j,    delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2,
    y_star=0,      delta_star=0,
    X_alpha=x_k-sum_j alpha_j y_j,
    z_pre=g_k,
    Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha)
      =2 sum_j alpha_j delta_j+||X_alpha-b_last z_pre||^2.       (1)

Norms and inner products in this display are Gram expressions, independent
of the choice of a vector realization. For fixed (b_last,alpha), (1) is
linear in C. For fixed C, it is convex jointly in (b_last,alpha), because
its vector inside the squared norm is affine and its other term is linear.
The function is continuous in all finite coordinates. Moreover delta_j>=0
follows from Q_(star,j)=-delta_j<=0.

## 2. The exact hull formula, with its proof

The maximal prefix interpolant in the cited fact is F_C=H_C^*, where

    H_C(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(j in J_pre)(<y_j,v>-delta_j).

For every x in the realizing Euclidean space,

    2F_C(x)=min_(alpha in Delta_pre)
                  [2 sum_j alpha_j delta_j
                    +||x-sum_j alpha_j y_j||^2].              (2)

Here is a direct proof, including attainment. Let v=grad F_C(x), the unique
maximizer of <x,v>-H_C(v) supplied by the predecessor. Let A be the nonempty
set of indices maximizing <y_j,v>-delta_j. One has

    x-v in conv{y_j:j in A}.                                  (3)

To prove this without a subgradient formula, suppose (3) fails. Separation
from the finite convex hull gives a vector d such that
<x-v,d> > max_(j in A)<y_j,d>. For small positive epsilon, the right
derivative of the maximum of the affine functions at v in direction d is
max_(j in A)<y_j,d>: the finitely many strictly inactive indices remain
inactive for all sufficiently small epsilon. The right derivative of
<x,v+epsilon d>-H_C(v+epsilon d) is therefore positive. This contradicts
maximality of v. Thus choose alpha supported on A with
x-v=sum alpha_j y_j. Its value in the right side of (2) equals 2F_C(x),
because the weighted active affine values equal their maximum and

    F_C(x)=<x,v>-||v||^2/2
              -sum alpha_j(<y_j,v>-delta_j)
          =||v||^2/2+sum alpha_j delta_j.

Conversely, for any alpha, H_C(u)>=||u||^2/2+
<sum alpha_j y_j,u>-sum alpha_j delta_j for every u. Maximizing
<x,u>-H_C(u) gives the upper bound in (2). The constructed active alpha
attains it. For ANY minimizing alpha in (2), completing the square gives

    grad F_C(x)=x-sum_j alpha_j y_j.                           (4)

Indeed equality in the upper bound at the unique conjugate maximizer
requires precisely that equality of vectors; alternatively strict
convexity in the barycenter proves that all minimizing barycenters agree.

The predecessor says F_C interpolates the entire prefix, is convex and
1-smooth, and is pointwise maximal among all such interpolants. Therefore

    W_n(h_pre,b_last)
       =max_(C in C_pre) min_(alpha in Delta_pre)
                           Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha).          (5)

For the <= direction, any original function produces a prefix in C_pre
and is bounded above at the resulting terminal point by F_C. For the >=
direction, F_C is itself legal and has the prescribed prefix and terminal
position. If ||x_0||<1, its unnormalized value is bounded by W times
||x_0||^2, and scaling every vector and every value gives the converse
normalization at any positive maximizer. A zero initial vector gives zero
data by the recursion. Maxima exist by compactness and (2).

Each such maximizing function lives in dimension at most n+1, the number
of prefix formal vectors. All information at the unqueried terminal is
constructed in this same space. No queried-prefix inequality is removed.

## 3. An elementary compact minimax lemma

The following finite-dimensional argument will be used here, rather than
an unexplained imported minimax theorem. Let C and A be nonempty compact
convex sets and let P(c,a) be continuous, affine in c, and convex in a.
Then

    max_c min_a P(c,a)=min_a max_c P(c,a),                      (6)

and both extrema are attained. To prove this, put m=max_c min_a P(c,a).
For each c let K_c={a in A:P(c,a)<=m}. Suppose some finite collection
c_1,...,c_s has empty intersection of these sets. The set

    U={u in R^s: some a in A has u_i>=P(c_i,a)-m for all i}

is convex, by convexity in a, and closed, by compactness of A. It does not
contain zero. It has a closest point u_* to zero, with ||u_*||>0. Since
U is invariant under addition of any vector with nonnegative coordinates,
the projection inequality <u_*,u-u_*>>=0 for u in U implies every coordinate
of u_* is nonnegative. Let p_i=(u_*)_i/sum_j(u_*)_j. These are nonnegative
and sum to one. Evaluating the same projection inequality at
u=(P(c_i,a)-m)_i gives, for every a,

    sum_i p_i P(c_i,a)
      >=m+||u_*||^2/sum_i(u_*)_i > m.

But c_bar=sum_i p_i c_i belongs to C, and the left side is P(c_bar,a).
This contradicts the definition of m. Thus the closed sets K_c have the
finite intersection property in compact A and have a common point a_*.
It satisfies max_c P(c,a_*)<=m. The reverse inequality is the direct
min-max inequality. Continuity and compactness give all stated extrema.

Applying (6) to (5) gives the exact terminal-weight minimax formula

    W_n(h_pre,b_last)
      =min_(alpha in Delta_pre) max_(C in C_pre)
                           Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha).          (7)

This retains the full prefix Gram matrix. It does not identify that state
with any scalar statistic of the prefix schedule.

## 4. Unbounded final steps, attainment, and one legal saddle function

Define

    rho_last(h_pre)=inf_(b_last>=0) W_n(h_pre,b_last).

The value function in b_last is continuous. This follows from (5), since
Phi_term is uniformly continuous on C_pre times Delta_pre times any
bounded closed stepsize interval. It is convex: in (7), the maximum over
C of the jointly convex functions in (1) is convex, and partial
minimization over the convex simplex preserves convexity. Equivalently,
(5) is a maximum over C of the convex functions 2F_C(x_k-b_last g_k).

The outer infimum is attained at a finite b_last. For a direct coercivity
check choose

    theta=1/[2(1+sum_(t<k)h_t)],
    P_theta=product_(t<k)(1-theta h_t)>0.

The scalar legal function f(x)=theta x^2/2 starting at one yields

    W_n(h_pre,b_last)>=theta P_theta^2(1-theta b_last)^2,

which tends to infinity. Continuity now gives an attained minimum.
Its value is positive: for each finite full schedule the cited fact proves
W_n>0 (or choose a sufficiently small positive curvature avoiding all
finitely many roots). Positivity at an attained minimizer gives rho_last>0.

The exact unbounded minimax relation is

    rho_last(h_pre)
      =max_(C in C_pre) min_(b_last>=0, alpha in Delta_pre)
                              Phi_term(C,b_last,alpha).       (8)

Here are all compactness details. For each positive integer B, apply (6)
to C_pre and [0,B] times Delta_pre. Let rho_B be the common value and
let C_B maximize the inner minimum. The values rho_B decrease to
rho_last by (7). Along a subsequence with B tending to infinity,
C_B converges to C_* in compact C_pre. For every fixed finite b_last
and every fixed alpha, the defining inequality
Phi_term(C_B,b_last,alpha)>=rho_B holds for all sufficiently large B.
Passing to the limit proves Phi_term(C_*,b_last,alpha)>=rho_last for
every pair. This and the direct min-max inequality prove (8).

Choose ANY optimal finite b_* and any alpha_* attaining (7) there.
Then

    Phi_term(C,b_*,alpha_*)<=rho_last
      =Phi_term(C_*,b_*,alpha_*)
      <=Phi_term(C_*,b_last,alpha)                             (9)

for every feasible C,b_last,alpha. The equality follows by applying the
two inequalities to each other at the middle point. Thus an actual saddle
pair exists without a strictly feasible Gram hypothesis and without any
compactness assumption on the last-step ray.

By (2), the SINGLE maximal prefix interpolant F_(C_*) satisfies

    min_(b_last>=0) 2F_(C_*)(x_k-b_last g_k)
      =2F_(C_*)(x_k-b_* g_k)=rho_last.                        (10)

Its initial norm is one: if it were smaller and nonzero, scaling its
prefix to norm one would scale its positive inner minimum upward,
contradicting (8). If the initial vector were zero, the inner value would
be zero. The constructed function needs dimension at most n+1.

Consequently, in the ORIGINAL normalization and arbitrary finite
dimensions, with the first n-1 GD steps fixed in advance,

    inf_(b>=0) W_n(h_pre,b)
      =sup_(legal f,x_0,x_star)
          inf_(b>=0) 2[f(x_k-(b/L)grad f(x_k))-f(x_star)]
                           /(L||x_0-x_star||^2).             (11)

The supremum on the right is attained by the function in (10), after
normalization. Its inner infimum is attained at b_*. For an arbitrary
legal f, maximal extension bounds its values at EVERY point of this
ray by F_C for the same prefix, so its inner infimum is bounded by the
right side of (8). Conversely F_(C_*) attains (8). This proves (11)
directly; it does not exchange a stepsize with the changing earlier GD
queries. Thus optimizing the final fixed step has exactly the unrestricted
worst-case value of allowing exact line search for that final step.

## 5. Explicit geometry and a consequence for the complete dual set

The inner minimum in (8) has the closed form

    min_(alpha in Delta_pre) [2sum alpha_j delta_j+||X_alpha||^2
                      -(<X_alpha,z_pre>_+)^2/||z_pre||^2]    (12)

when z_pre!=0; the fraction is replaced by zero when z_pre=0. This is
obtained by minimizing a scalar quadratic in b_last, whose minimizing
step is <X_alpha,z_pre>_+/||z_pre||^2. Here t_+=max(t,0).
For z_pre=0, the actual point x_k is a minimizer of F_C and the inner
value is zero (also choose alpha_k=1). Thus z_pre!=0 at C_*.

Let v_*=grad F_(C_*)(x_k-b_* z_pre). Since the value in (10) is positive,
v_*!=0. The minimizing last step is interior: at b=0 the derivative of
F_(C_*)(x_k-b z_pre) is -||z_pre||^2<0. Differentiability and (10) give

    <v_*,z_pre>=0.                                           (13)

Adding the two interpolation inequalities between x_k and its terminal
point gives
<z_pre-v_*,b_* z_pre>>=||z_pre-v_*||^2. Therefore

    b_* >= 1+||v_*||^2/||z_pre||^2 > 1.                     (14)

This holds for EVERY minimizing last step, since any one of them can be
paired with C_* in (9). The outgoing terminal-to-k interpolation inequality
also gives

    delta_k >= rho_last/2+||v_*||^2/2 > 0.                 (15)

Indeed its gradient-position term is b_*<v_*,z_pre>=0, and
||v_*-z_pre||^2=||v_*||^2+||z_pre||^2.

Now take ANY tight full nonnegative-flow PSD certificate at the schedule
(h_pre,b_*), not necessarily one with the terminal support selected in
the predecessor. Evaluate its full quadratic identity on the actual
normalized trajectory of F_(C_*). The objective gap equals its certificate
level rho_last, every Q_ij is nonpositive, and the quadratic is PSD.
Thus every nonnegative multiplier satisfies complementarity. Since
Q_(star,k)=-delta_k is strictly negative by (15), EVERY such certificate
has

    lambda_(star,k)=0.                                       (16)

For certificates with no incoming terminal multipliers, the accepted
square identity gives v_*=V_alpha. Their formal last-step matrix derivative
evaluated on this same transcript is 2<z_pre,v_*>=0. Thus one common
actual primal supplies last-step stationarity for every selected optimal
certificate. No corresponding earlier-cut stationarity is claimed.

## 6. A complete two-dimensional test and the role of dimension

Take n=1 and in R^2 choose

    x_0=(1,0),
    z_pre=(1/3,sqrt(2)/6),
    y_0=x_0-z_pre=(2/3,-sqrt(2)/6),
    delta_0=1/6,    f_0=1/4.

Then ||z_pre||^2=1/6, <x_0,z_pre>=1/3, and the two prefix inequalities
are Q_(0,star)=0 and Q_(star,0)=-1/6. Define the fixed legal function by

    F(x)=[v -> ||v||^2/2+max(0,<y_0,v>-1/6)]^*(x).

The cited fact proves that it is convex and 1-smooth, interpolates the
stated initial jet, and has minimum zero at the origin. Formula (2) is

    2F(x)=min_(0<=alpha<=1) [alpha/3+||x-alpha y_0||^2].

At b=3/2 and alpha=1/2, the residual is

    v_*=(1/6,-sqrt(2)/6),
    ||v_*||^2=1/12,
    <z_pre,v_*>=0,
    <y_0,v_*>=1/6.

Both derivatives of the jointly convex quadratic
alpha/3+||x_0-b z_pre-alpha y_0||^2 vanish at this pair. It is feasible,
so its global minimum on b>=0, alpha in [0,1] is 1/6+1/12=1/4.
This is an explicit function whose normalized error after exact line
search is 1/4.

For the matching universal upper bound at b=3/2, all unlisted multipliers
zero, set lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1. They have the
correct nonnegative flow, and on the FULL formal space

    (1/4)||x_0||^2-2f_1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
           =||(1/2)x_0-g_0-g_1||^2.

This proves the exact value rho_last(empty)=1/4 without an external
one-step performance theorem.

In dimension ONE, by contrast, exact line search from any nonoptimal
point reaches a minimizer: the derivative has the same sign as its
displacement from any chosen minimizer, so the nonnegative negative-
gradient ray contains that minimizer. If the derivative is zero, the
point is already a minimizer. This applies after ANY fixed prefix.
Hence the scalar supremum of the exact-line-search error is zero.
The positive value (11) therefore depends essentially on permitting
higher finite dimensions; its minimax interchange must not be applied
to a rank-one-restricted Gram set, which is not convex.

## 7. Scope, literature audit, and unresolved bridge

This candidate derives (2), (7), and (11) together with the explicit
attained saddle and all boundary details in a single proof. Its only
verified building block is 1252b8823bb5a290. All additional arguments are
included here; none is being silently promoted from an earlier finding.

For background the Matlas convex-conjugacy/minimax query returned generic
duality results, none needed as a proof premise. Maurice Sion's original
"On general minimax theorems", Pacific J. Math. 8 (1958), pp.171-176,
especially Section3, was downloaded and its definitions and proof read
through the browser's PDF extraction. Its broader one-compact-set theorem
is consistent with this finite convex setting, but Section3 above supplies
the particular elementary separation proof used here, and Section4 supplies
the unbounded-ray limit and attainment separately. No paper theorem is
used as a black box.

The full queried-prefix state remains in C_pre. Earlier steps change this
feasible set, so (11) cannot be iterated backward as a scalar recurrence.
Neither (12) nor (16) establishes Huber tightness, eliminates remote forward
prefix edges, or proves the Zhang--Jiang exact-profile lower bound. The next
target-facing question is whether the geometric inner value (12) admits
a useful lower bound under a realizable, explicitly constructed family
of full prefixes. Its dimension-two one-step witness shows why restricting
that question to scalar exact-line-search adversaries would lose everything.

# One full equality-chart search: derivation and frozen plan

Status: joint UNVERIFIED research derivation and numerical-discovery plan.
Owner max3, continuation round after completed44, ceiling45/attempted46.
Authority: current TASK.md and master guidance59c43473b63b1939. Exactly
ONE mathematical job,30 conservatively charged seconds; no retry, formal
gate, installation, consult, native agent, or automatic renewal.

The original exact-profile Goal, W=2R, and the sufficient threshold in
accepted4c92d3fc4eb6153e are unchanged. The numerical target is an actual
length5 word with A>Gamma5=7977607/806000, preferably A>=99/10. A numerical
endpoint is only a candidate. No seed or original-target theorem is asserted.

## 1. Correctness inputs and reproduced equality chart

The accepted inputs are04219554e41d9750, Section10, and1252b8823bb5a290,
proof paragraph1. They give the original finite interpolation orientation
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0 and the certificate identity

 V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_5)>,
 V_h=2sum_(i<5)h_i(f_i-f_5)+sum_(i<5)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -s(s+1)||g_5||^2,
 s=sum h_i, A=1+s,
 T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
 L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_4,2s)^T,
 D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<5,-s(s+2)), M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2,

provided L^T e=omega. All six gradients are independent formal variables,
e is the six-vector of ones, and Lambda has zero diagonal. All30 original
ordered off-diagonal coefficients must be nonnegative, and M must be PSD
on the whole R^6. The coefficient identity is also checked by separating
position terms sym(L(T-I)) and squared-gradient terms sym(L)-diag(omega)/2.
Adding the diagonal part of V_h yields exactly D+sym(LT).

Put z=Te, Z=diag(z), d(X)=Xe-X^Te, and d_z(X)=Xz-ZX^Te=d(XZ).
Both divergences have total sum zero. Direct coordinate calculation gives
2De+T^T omega=-Z omega: if p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, its i<5 coordinate is
2h_i(2-h_i)-2h_i+2h_i(p_i+h_i)=2h_i(1+p_i)=-z_i omega_i.
The last coordinate is -2s(s+2)+2s=-2s(s+1).
Thus, when d(Lambda)=omega,

 2Me=Lz-Zomega=-d_z(Lambda).

Consequently the two original equalities are equivalent to
d(Lambda)=omega and d_z(Lambda)=0. Moreover, under the first equality,
e^TMe=0 because e^TDe=-(s^2+sum h_i^2) and z^Tomega=s^2+sum h_i^2.
If M is PSD, the quadratic (e+tv)^TM(e+tv) has zero constant term;
nonnegativity for both signs of t forces e^TMv=0 for all v. Hence Me=0
is necessary for every original feasible certificate and costs no solutions.

Use the path v=(0,1,2,5,4,3), oriented successively. For edge u->w let
S be the initial path segment through u, Delta=z_u-z_w, and N have all20
independent off-tree ordered entries, zero diagonal and tree entries.
Define

 alpha=sum_(i in S)omega_i-sum_(i in S,j outside S)(N_ij-N_ji),
 beta=-sum_(i in S,j outside S)(N_ij z_j-N_ji z_i).
 Lambda_uw=(z_u alpha-beta)/Delta,
 Lambda_wu=(z_w alpha-beta)/Delta,
 Lambda_ij=N_ij off the tree.

This solves a-b=alpha and z_w a-z_u b=beta. Every tree cut of each
residual divergence is zero. Rooting the tree, a leaf residual is zero
by its singleton subtree cut; subtracting zero child subtree sums proves
each parent residual zero, and the root follows from total sum zero.
Conversely every original equality array must satisfy these two cut
equations, whose determinant is -Delta, so its dependent entries are
uniquely the displayed formulas. The inverse just reads the20 off-tree
entries. They and all five h_i remain independent variables.

The successive cuts are {0},{0,1},{0,1,2},{0,1,2,5},{0,1,2,5,4}; their
Delta values are -h_0,-h_1,-(h_2+h_3+h_4),h_4,h_3. They are nonzero in
the registered box. This is a coordinate basis, not a support assumption.
At a zero Delta the actual compatibility condition is beta=z_u alpha;
if compatible the whole pair is (t+alpha,t), otherwise no completion
exists at those off-tree coordinates. These singular cases lie outside
this search box and are not declared infeasible for the original problem.
In particular a box restriction is not a global theorem about the cone.

## 2. Full-space factor equivalence, including singular factors

Write B for the leading5-by-5 block of M and J=[I_5;-1_5^T]. From Me=0
one obtains all final row and column entries, giving M=JBJ^T. For arbitrary
independent g_0,...,g_5 its quadratic form is
(g_(0:4)-g_5 1)^TB(g_(0:4)-g_5 1). It follows in both directions that
M>=0 iff B>=0. This identity does not identify any two gradients.

A symmetric PSD B has a lower triangular factor C with nonnegative
diagonal, B=CC^T, including the singular case. Induct on the first pivot
of the current PSD residual. A positive pivot p allows a square completion
and leaves the PSD Schur residual. If p=0, positivity of each two-variable
quadratic forces its whole off-diagonal row zero; set that factor column
zero and continue on the remaining principal submatrix. A negative pivot,
or zero pivot with nonzero off-diagonal row, is incompatible with PSD.
Conversely any CC^T is PSD. This establishes exact equivalence without
fixing rank. The full factor is F=JC; its last row is minus the sum of
the first five rows. All15 lower factor entries vary in the search.

## 3. Reproducing the specified strict start, without adopting a draft fact

Let a=sqrt2, rho=a-1, lambda=1+a,
b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2, p=a+b, t=99/100, delta=1/100,
X=ta, Y=tb, S=X+Y, C0=1+S, d=rho C0-delta,
h_*=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y), A_*=2C0+d,
c=(C0+d)/(C0-d), k=1+2c,
tau=delta(2a C0-delta)/(C0-d), eta=2tau,
u=2S(1+X)/Y, v0=2SC0/Y, epsilon=1/10000000.
Here C0 is a scalar capacity, C above is a matrix factor, and v0 is a
scalar coefficient, distinct from the path v.
The identity b(a+b)=2(1+a) follows by squaring the defining root.

The two-step child (X,Y) has multipliers Lambda_01=2,
Lambda_10=2(1+X), Lambda_12=u, Lambda_21=v0, other entries zero.
Its flow is (-2X,-2Y,2S), since v0-u=2S. Direct substitution gives the
path-Laplacian remainder with edge weights

 w0=2-X^2=2delta(1+t),
 w1=(S/Y)[2-Y^2+(2-Y)X]=2(p/b)delta(1+lambda t).

For two such children and the bridge1+d, the complete baseline array is

 [0,       2,       0,       0,          0,         0]
 [2(1+X),  0,       u,       0,          0,         0]
 [0,       v0,      0,       0,          0,         2cd+eta]
 [0,       0,       2cX,     0,          2k,        2cX]
 [0,       0,       2cY,     2k(1+X),    0,         ku+2cY]
 [0,       0,       2c+eta,  0,          kv0,       0].

Its last four divergences are respectively
v0-u+2c(d-C0)=-2(1+d), 4cX-2kX=-2X,
4cY+2kX+k(u-v0)=-2Y, k(v0-u)+2c(2-C0-d)=2s.
The first two are the child divergences. Thus all six flows are checked.

Here is a full quadratic check of its remainder. Write
U_L=g0-g1, V_L=g1-g2, U_R=g3-g4, V_R=g4-g5,
zeta=g2-g5, Kvec=X U_R+S V_R.
Expanding the original matrix, using c(C0-d)=C0+d and
d(2C0+d)-C0^2=-delta(2a C0-delta), gives

 M0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
       +k(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
       +tau[(C0-d)||zeta||^2-2<zeta,Kvec>].

One way to check every mixed term is to use
E=XQ_32+YQ_42+Q_52+dQ_25+XQ_35+YQ_45.
The tail-to-tail terms cancel between its two tail sums; bridge-to-tail
terms cancel against dQ_25; tail-to-terminal terms against Q_52; the
bridge-to-terminal terms in Q_52 and dQ_25 cancel. Its remaining gradient
part is X(1-X)||g3||^2+Y(1-Y)||g4||^2+
[(C0-d)/2-d^2]||g2||^2+(C0+d)||g5||^2/2.
The identity V_h=V_left+kV_right-2cE+
c[d(2C0+d)-C0^2](||g5||^2-||g2||^2) follows by matching its values and
diagonals. Adding eta(Q_25+Q_52), with
Q_25+Q_52=-d||zeta||^2-(C0+d)<zeta,g5>-<zeta,Kvec>, gives exactly the
displayed M0[g]. Thus the full matrix, rather than only its diagonal,
has been reproduced.

Put R=X^2/w0+S^2/w1=Rbar/delta,
Rbar=t^2[1/(1+t)+lambda/(1+lambda t)], q=tau/(C0-d), sigma=k-qR,
E0=zeta-Kvec/(C0-d). Square completion gives the exact identity

 M0[g]=w0||U_L||^2+w1||V_L||^2
 +sigma(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)
 +q||S sqrt(w0/w1)U_R-X sqrt(w1/w0)V_R||^2
 +tau(C0-d)||E0||^2.

The cross term in the fourth square is -2qXS<U_R,V_R>, and its other
terms equal q[R(w0||U_R||^2+w1||V_R||^2)-||Kvec||^2], verifying the
identity. For quantitative bounds, the increasing polynomial
F(x)=x^2+ax-2(1+a) changes sign between8/5 and161/100, while
7/5<a<71/50. Hence3<p<303/100,397/100<C0<403/100,
1<X<71/50,1<Y<161/100,3/2<d<17/10,39/100<d/C0<21/50,
(29/50)C0<C0-d<(61/100)C0<3.
Both terms in Rbar increase for t>=0, so Rbar<=lambda/2.
Consequently k>339/61>11/2 and
qR<(7/2)C0/[(29/50)^2 C0^2]=8750/(841C0)<3, giving sigma>5/2.
Also w0,w1>3delta, sigma w0,sigma w1>(15/2)delta, and
tau(C0-d)>11delta. The bounds X/(C0-d)<2/3 and S/(C0-d)<4/3 imply
||zeta||^2<=3||E0||^2+(4/3)||U_R||^2+(16/3)||V_R||^2.
Therefore M0[g]>=delta E_tree, where E_tree is the sum of the five
squared differences along(0,1,2,5,4,3).
For sum g_i=0, ||g||^2=(1/6)sum_(i<j)(g_i-g_j)^2. Bounding each pair
along that path gives edge coefficients15,24,27,24,15, hence
||g||^2<=(9/2)E_tree and M0>=(2delta/9)P on e-perp,
where P=I-ee^T/6. This proves the baseline margin directly.

To fill every missing multiplier, let K0=6I-ee^T and
F_l=sum_(i in S_l)(K0z)_i, w_l=F_l/(z_u-z_w), gamma_l=1-w_l.
Set H=K0-sum_l w_l(e_u-e_w)(e_u-e_w)^T. Then He=0.
Also Hz=0: at vertex v_l the edge sum has coordinate F_l-F_(l-1),
which is (K0z)_(v_l), with F_-1=F_5=0. Add epsilon on both directions
of every non-tree pair and epsilon gamma_l on both directions of each
tree pair. This changes the Laplacian by epsilon H, preserving both
flow and Me=0. The full changed remainder is M=M0+epsilon sym(HT).

For an explicit construction let Zsum=2+C0+3A_*-2Y. The five w_l are
(Zsum-6)/X, [2Zsum-6(2+X)]/Y,
[3Zsum-6(2+X+C0)]/(C0+d),
[6(2+X+C0+A_*)-4Zsum]/Y,
[Zsum-6(C0+1+d)]/X.
These specify every entry of the dense array by the preceding additions.
Each cut has at most9 crossing pairs, each potential difference has
absolute value<s<9, and each tree difference has magnitude>1. Thus
|w_l|<81, |gamma_l|<82. Every original tree coefficient is at least2;
after filling, all30 coefficients are >=epsilon. The direct bounds
c<5/2,k<6,tau<5delta,u<10,v0<16 give all final coefficients<97.
Moreover ||H||<816 and ||T||<=||T||_F<sqrt141<12, so
||sym(HT)||<10000. Since epsilon=delta/100000,
M>(2delta/9-delta/10)P>(delta/9)P=(1/900)P on e-perp.
The upper bound ||M0||<342delta follows from four edge forms bounded
by252delta and the bridge form bounded by90delta; adding the perturbation
gives M<(7/2)P. For g=(y,0), ||Pg||^2>=||y||^2/6, so B>=I/5400.
No numerical evaluation has been used in this derivation.

The chart's starting coordinates are only
N_32=N_35=2cX+epsilon, N_42=2cY+epsilon, all other17 equal epsilon.
The baseline cut alpha values are -2X,-2S,2c(d-1),2kS-2cY,2kX;
the beta values are0,0,2cC0S,2c(C0S+A_*X),2cX(C0+A_*).
Filling changes beta by -epsilon Delta_l gamma_l and leaves alpha
unchanged. Substitution into the cut formulas reproduces all ten tree
entries of the explicit dense array. This proves exact chart readback.
The initial numerical job must additionally compare these independently
constructed arrays and check the actual original matrix and margins.

Its capacity A_*=a+(99/100)[(1+a)(a+b)+1] is below48763/5000<49/5<Gamma5.
It is the required strict start, not a usable improving seed.

## 4. Frozen numerical specification

Implementation: run.wl, executed ONCE by the existing Wolfram evaluator.
All machine mathematics, including model construction, initial arrays,
factorization, optimization, original readbacks, and encoding, occurs in
that single call. No code is evaluated for calibration beforehand.

Variables: five independent h; all20 off-tree ordered coefficients in
lexicographic order; all15 entries of a lower triangular5-by-5 factor C.
The ten dependent Lambda entries use the exact cuts above. No reflection,
support, repeated row, zero remainder, fixed rank, or one-parameter path
is imposed. The relation h=(X,Y,1+d,X,Y) holds ONLY at the initial point.
Equality constraints are the15 lower triangular entries of B-CC^T=0.
All30 multiplier inequalities remain imposed, including tree entries.

Search box:1/4<=each h_i<=6;0<=each original Lambda_ij<=400 (i!=j);
-100<=each lower factor entry<=100, diagonal>=0; additionally every entry
of the FULL6-by-5 factor JC is in[-100,100]. These are search restrictions,
not proved global bounds. The registered objective is maximize1+sum h_i.

Method: one FindMinimum call on its negative, Method->InteriorPoint,
WorkingPrecision->MachinePrecision, AccuracyGoal->8, PrecisionGoal->8,
MaxIterations->600. Initial point is exactly the algebraic specification
above evaluated at machine precision; its factor is obtained from the
actual positive definite leading block. No jitter, alternate start, SDP,
repair, grid, precision rerun or second optimization is permitted.

Initial abort tests: finite real arrays of the correct dimensions; full
flow, Me, direct-array/chart comparison and full factor residual <=1e-9;
all30 coefficients>=epsilon(1-1e-6); full minimum eigenvalue>=-1e-9;
complement minimum>=1/900-1e-9; leading minimum>=1/5400-1e-9; all search
boxes and factor diagonal signs. Failure aborts without optimization.

Numerical candidate tests: actual original coefficient signs >=0;
full flow and Me infinity norms<=1e-7; full original/factor residual<=1e-7;
full minimum eigenvalue>=-1e-8; original/model matrix discrepancy<=1e-8;
factor diagonals>=0 and all search boxes. Stop early once such a point has
A>Gamma5+1e-6. All negative entries and eigenvalues remain visible. This
is only numerical credibility, never exact PSD or a certificate proof.
No exact reconstruction is planned in this job: complete arrays are saved
for a separately selected next stage if the candidate test succeeds.

Counters: EvaluationMonitor increments an exact callback counter and
throws at2000. StepMonitor increments an exact step counter and performs
one full ORIGINAL-model readback at every accepted step. It throws by598
steps, reserving two of the600 total readbacks for initial and final checks.
The solver's internal iterations also have MaxIterations600. The initial
and returned/final point are checked separately; the best checked point
is retained with its already computed readback, avoiding a redundant call.

Time: one30s charge made before evaluation, no refund. Model build and
optimizer phase has a23s kernel guard with a22s callback wall guard; full
readback and compressed export have an overall26.5s kernel guard and the
evaluator receives timeConstraint27. Reserve3s for local decoding and
persistence, enforced by a local SIGALRM. The core deadline is absolute
from call start, not renewed per iteration. Accounted cap is30 regardless
of early exit; actual kernel, transport wall, and local processing times
are recorded separately. Unknown-time timeout charges the full30 and
closes the ledger. The prior870s conservative reservation becomes900s;
neither number is represented as measured runtime.

Output: final full readback FIRST, then best feasible and initial arrays;
h,Lambda,M,C,JC,J and a fixed orthonormal complement basis; all original
flows, Me, factor and model residuals, signs and full/complement/leading
eigenvalues; counters, solver output, version, status and every received
warning. Objective history is explicitly SAMPLED: step1,every10th step,
and the final readback. Exact total and omitted counts are supplied.
There is no per-callback full-vector dump. The complete last callback
vector is retained as a trial point, without an unperformed readback.
Lossless gzip/base64 chunks preserve the complete bounded payload; raw
received response is saved before parsing. No clipping, truncation,
rounding-away of negative values, or reconstructed missing output.

## 5. Retrieval and authority limits

The shared COMPLETE draft841a70f2ad83841d was read in full and its SHA256
matched4e1f0afaa542bb54c0c4e047e72f9a50df6e60011718bd2a1305b8ffa6f4c6b6.
The derivation above independently reproduces the needed formulas from
accepted originals; the shared draft is not promoted to a fact.
The known exact local BnB-PEP paper2203.07305v5 was read at Lemma3 and
Algorithm1 Stage2 for the factor/interior-point method analogy. No external
theorem is imported. Matlas hits on generalized eigenvalues/nonnegative
rank were unrelated. Official Wolfram FindMinimum and TimeConstrained
documentation was checked. No branch-and-bound or global optimum claim
is made. All new algebra remains unverified because the current task
expressly forbids fact_submit. Failure of this run cannot prove full-cone
nonexistence or settle the original problem.

   zz = tt.ee; om = Join[-2 h, {2 Total[h]}]; la = nm;
   Do[
    {uedge, vedge} = tree[[k]]; cut = cuts[[k]];
    outside = Complement[Range[6], cut];

                                                {i, cut}, {j, outside}]]];

    den = zz[[uedge]] - zz[[vedge]];
    la[[uedge, vedge]] = (zz[[uedge]] aa - bt)/den;
    la[[vedge, uedge]] = (zz[[vedge]] aa - bt)/den,
    {k, 5}];
   lf = DiagonalMatrix[la.ee] - la;

    "sampled_history_columns" -> {"step", "callback", "seconds", "capacity", "min_Lambda",
       "flow_inf", "null_inf", "full_min_eigenvalue", "factor_inf", "numerically_feasible"},
    "sampled_history" -> history, "sampled_step_rows" -> Length[history],
    "unsampled_step_count" -> iter - Length[history],
    "final_history_row" -> If[TrueQ[initialPassed] && AssociationQ[finalRead] &&
                  TrueQ[finalRead["finite_real"]], row[iter, evals, finalRead], Null],
    "maximum_trial_capacity_not_feasibility_claim" -> maxTrialCapacity,
    "last_evaluation_vector_unchecked_if_distinct_from_final" -> lastEval,
    "exact_reconstruction_attempted" -> exactAttempted,
    "model_build_seconds" -> modelBuildElapsed,
    "initial_checks_complete_seconds_from_start" -> initialElapsed,
    "optimizer_seconds" -> optimizationElapsed,
    "kernel_seconds_before_export" -> preExportElapsed|>;
  exportStarted = AbsoluteTime[];
  payload = BaseEncode[ExportByteArray[ExportString[result, "RawJSON", "Compact" -> True], {"GZIP", "Text"}]];
  chunks = StringPartition[payload, UpTo[3000]];
  Do[Print["EQ45_CHUNK_", k, "_OF_", Length[chunks], "=", chunks[[k]]], {k, Length[chunks]}];
  kernelElapsed = AbsoluteTime[] - started;
  ExportString[<|"status" -> status, "initial_passed" -> initialPassed,
      "best_feasible_A" -> If[bestRead === Null, Null, bestA],
      "optimizer_invocations" -> calls, "evaluation_callback_count" -> evals,
      "step_monitor_count" -> iter, "original_readback_count" -> reads,
      "export_chunks" -> Length[chunks], "encoding" -> "base64-gzip-UTF8-JSON",
      "kernel_seconds_including_payload_export" -> kernelElapsed,
      "payload_export_seconds" -> AbsoluteTime[] - exportStarted,
      "accounted_seconds" -> 30|>, "RawJSON", "Compact" -> True],
  26.5,
  ExportString[<|"status" -> "overall_kernel_timeout", "initial_passed" -> initialPassed,
      "optimizer_invocations" -> calls, "evaluation_callback_count" -> evals,
      "step_monitor_count" -> iter, "original_readback_count" -> reads,
      "kernel_messages" -> ToString[$MessageList, InputForm],
      "kernel_seconds" -> AbsoluteTime[] - started, "accounted_seconds" -> 30,
      "complete_arrays_available" -> False|>, "RawJSON", "Compact" -> True]]
]]

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\shared_evidence\4339b7cebe8d6905.md

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002990 -->
# Extra storage from shortening the primitive pair

Status: complete candidate identity, not verifier-accepted. This is a proposed
input for the remaining joint boundary transport, not an improved schedule.
No solver or verifier call is used.

For 0<=u<=3/2 consider the physical pair h=(u,u). Write
p=g_0, m=g_1, q=g_2 and define

    K=1+2u,
    V=2u(f_0+f_1-2f_2)+u(1-u)(||p||^2+||m||^2)
        -2u(1+2u)||q||^2,
    epsilon=(3/2-u)(u+1/2)>=0,
    I_ij=f_j-f_i-<g_i,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

On samples of one convex 1-smooth function I_ij>=0. The following exact
identity retains two independent gradient differences:

    V=I_02+(1+2u)I_10+(3+4u)I_12+(4+8u)I_21
       +(1/4)||p+m-2q||^2
       +epsilon[||p-m||^2+4||m-q||^2].               (1)

All four actual interpolation coefficients are nonnegative, and every square
coefficient is nonnegative in the stated range. Thus this is a full candidate
primitive certificate for the pair. At u=3/2 it reduces to the existing pair
identity in finding b750ce63e2821fa8; the epsilon term is the new retained
slack from changing the physical steps, not the proof weights.

For completeness, here is a coefficient proof of (1). Use x_1=x_0-u p and
x_2=x_0-u(p+m). Then

    I_02=f_2-f_0+(u-1/2)||p||^2+u<p,m>+<p,q>-(1/2)||q||^2,
    I_10=f_0-f_1-(1/2)||p||^2-(1/2)||m||^2+(1-u)<p,m>,
    I_12=f_2-f_1+(u-1/2)||m||^2+<m,q>-(1/2)||q||^2,
    I_21=f_1-f_2-(1/2)||m||^2-(1/2)||q||^2+(1-u)<m,q>.

The weighted sum in (1) has function coefficients(2u,2u,-4u). Subtracting
its gradient part from V leaves a quadratic form on a=p-q and b=m-q:

    (a,b)^T R_u(a,b),
    R_u=[[alpha,1/2-alpha],[1/2-alpha,5alpha-1]],
    alpha=1+u-u^2=1/4+epsilon.

Therefore

    R_u=(1/4)[[1,1],[1,1]]+epsilon[[1,-1],[-1,5]],

which is the sum of squares in (1). Its determinant is
4alpha^2-1/4 and its trace is6alpha-1. At u<3/2 it is positive definite,
because alpha>1/4; at u=3/2 it has rank1. More explicitly,
[[1,-1],[-1,5]] has determinant4 and positive upper-left entry1, so its
quadratic form is ||a-b||^2+4||b||^2.

## Quadratic slack and the precise changed hypothesis

The pair's quadratic response is Pi_u=(1-u)^2. Its quadratic-balance ratio is

    kappa(u)=K Pi_u=(1+2u)(1-u)^2.

For 0<u<3/2, kappa(u)<1, since

    1-kappa(u)=u^2(3-2u)>0.

At u=3/2, kappa=1. If u=3/2-delta with delta small and positive,

    K=4-2delta,
    kappa=1-(9/2)delta+6delta^2-2delta^3,
    epsilon=2delta-delta^2.

Thus shortening the pair supplies explicit positive gradient-variation
storage and strictly weakens the quadratic product test. The unequal-block
obstruction requiring exact quadratic balance cannot be applied to these
inputs without changing its hypotheses.

For three equal copies with bridges1+cK and1+dK, put M=3+c+d. The two
necessary cut tests remain

    Mc<=2+d, Md<=2+c,

while the quadratic test becomes

    Mcd<=kappa(u)^(-3)                              (2)

when kappa(u)>0; at u=1 the quadratic response is zero and this test is
vacuous. Passing (2) is not sufficient for a primitive certificate. It only
identifies a concrete unresolved regime not closed by the balanced result.

## Joint objective and primitive-dual storage

There is also an exact correlated-slack identity throughout the explicit
interval 29/20<=u<=3/2. Define

    H=2(f_0-f_2)-4u||q||^2
         -||p-(u(p+m)+q)/K||^2.

First, direct expansion gives the primitive-dual identity

    H=(4u+1)V/K^2+(2u/K)I_10+(2/K)I_20
                          +(4u^2/K^2)(I_02+I_12).   (3)

Together with (1), it proves H>=0 for this actual pair. More information is
retained by the following identity:

    V-(4/5)H
      =(2/5)I_01+(2u-6/5)I_10
        +(4u-4/5)I_12+(8u-12/5)I_21
        +(1/4)[P||z||^2+2D<z,w>+Q||w||^2],         (4)

where the ACTUAL gradient combinations and scalar coefficients are

    z=p+m-2q, w=p-m,
    P=(-20u^2+36u-8+4/K^2)/5,
    Q=4(3-2u)(5u-1)/5,
    D=-4(3-2u)(5u^2+u-1)/(5K).

The four interpolation coefficients in (4) are nonnegative on the stated
interval. The quadratic matrix (1/4)[[P,D],[D,Q]] is PSD, and is positive
definite when u<3/2. Here is an explicit check. Put delta=3/2-u, so
0<=delta<=1/20. Since K=4-2delta,

    P=[1+24delta-20delta^2+4/K^2]/5>=1/4.

For delta>0, Q>0 and

    D^2/Q=4(3-2u)(5u^2+u-1)^2/[5K^2(5u-1)]
          <=4418/38025 <1/4 <=P.

The displayed upper bound uses 3-2u<=1/10,
5u^2+u-1<=47/4, K>=39/10, and 5u-1>=25/4. Thus its determinant is strictly
positive. At delta=0, P=1/4 and D=Q=0, leaving ||z||^2/16. This is exactly
the older u=3/2 correlated storage, and the second eigenvalue becomes
positive on shortening the physical steps.

For a full matrix on the distinct variables(p,m,q), set

    t11=(P+Q+2D)/4, t12=(P-Q)/4, t22=(P+Q-2D)/4.

Then the remainder in (4) is

    (p,m,q)^T J_u(p,m,q),
    J_u=[[t11,t12,-t11-t12],
         [t12,t22,-t12-t22],
         [-t11-t12,-t12-t22,t11+2t12+t22]].          (5)

It is PSD by (4); its kernel is the constant-gradient subspace when
u<3/2. Equivalently, one may replace m by d_block/u-p to carry the actual
displacement d_block=u(p+m). No initial or terminal gradient of a neighboring
block is identified with these variables.

For a coefficient audit of (4), the following two-parameter identity is
convenient. Choose scalars b0,e0 and put

    a0=2-2b0, c0=2u+2-b0-e0,
    d0=4+4u-b0-e0, f0=4+8u-2e0.

These lower-case subscripted letters in this audit are interpolation
coefficients, not function values or block capacities. The exact expansion is

    V=a0 I_01+b0 I_02+c0 I_10+d0 I_12+e0 I_20+f0 I_21
       +(p-q,m-q)^T R(p-q,m-q),

    R11=2-u^2-(1-u)b0,
    R22=6+4u-5u^2-(2-u)(b0+e0),
    2R12=-[4-2u^2+(2u-3)b0-(1-u)e0].

Set L0=20u^2+4u+1, b0=16u^2/L0 and e0=8K/L0. Substitute this expansion
in (1-(4/5)(4u+1)/K^2)V and then subtract the other terms of (4/5)H from
(3). The I_02 and I_20 coefficients cancel exactly; the four remaining
coefficients are those in (4). Rewriting the remainder using z,w gives P,D,Q
as displayed. These are algebraic identities, not appeals to an unverified
primitive residual lemma.

## What a next join must prove

The retained variables in (1) and (4) are the ACTUAL p,m,q of each block. Since
d_block=u(p+m), they can also be stored as(p,d_block,q) for u>0, without
identifying the next block's start gradient with the bridge gradient. An
attempt to spend the epsilon term across two bridges must preserve these
distinct variables and the four displayed interpolation coefficients. It
must finish with a nonnegative full matrix and an original-objective bound.
No such two-bridge completion or asymptotic improvement is proved here.
