To prove (4), use saturated values f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 on

    x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),
    x_aux =(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).

For these values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j). In ell times the base
plus mu times the auxiliary, the complete nonzero residual list is

    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre, Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,
    Q03=-c03old, Q23=-c23old.

Every other ordered residual is zero. In particular Q13 cancels
because ell=a^2*b*(1-bt); at Q03 use ell+ab(1+abt)=ab*u, and
at Q23 use ell+a^2*b^2*mu=a^2*b*beta. Telescoping (1) gives
R; the two gradient columns modulo e are -mu*B and Avec, proving (4).

For (5), start the scalar two-step cut at x0=A with gradients
(1,1,-b), then extend FORMALLY with constant gradient -b and affine
values through the last two steps. Its energy is -D and its column
modulo e is beta*J. Its negative residuals are
Q20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta, and its positive residuals are
Q03=Q13=w*b*beta, Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta. All others vanish.
Thus (1) gives

 D=b*beta*(w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14))
                        -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).

Besides (6), direct multiplication of T gives

    2<e1,B>_M=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(1+v)*Lambda14.

Adding these rows, and using (w+v)-w(1+v)=-vc, gives (5).

The additional ORIGINAL row used in this derivation is

    2<E,J+B>_M=-a*Lambda20+Lambda02+w*Lambda03+w*Lambda04.           (7)

In fact e2=e-J-B and
2M02=a*Lambda20-Lambda02-w*(Lambda03+Lambda04). This proves (7)
without any PSD quotient or new support assumption.


### Definition needed for the original positive root

For k>0 and real theta, set

    F(k,theta)=b^2*w^2-z*theta
          +(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta^2/4-k*ell*mu^2.

This definition and the original row identities just proved suffice for
the following derivation. No capped optimizer or eps=0 matching is used.

## 3. The unrestricted root and the new full-row remainder

Assume v>0 for now. Then ell>0, z>0 and 1-bt>0. Set

    k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), kold=b^2*w^2/(ell*mu^2).

The minimum of F(k,theta) over all real theta is attained at

    theta(k)=2*z*k*mu/(1+a^2*k*mu).

It is strictly decreasing as a function of k: at each fixed theta,
partial F/partial k=-ell*mu^2-theta^2/(4*k^2*mu)<0, and the minimum
is attained continuously. At k0 the minimum divided by mu^2 is
b^2*(gamma-1)*(gamma+a^2*k0*mu)/(1+a^2*k0*mu)>0,
where gamma=w/mu>1. At kold it is negative, since F(kold,0)=0 and
the negative linear term improves it for small positive theta.
There is therefore a unique root k in (k0,kold).

Here is an explicit definition requiring no root search. Define

    Hbar=(2*b*w-beta
          +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),
    k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), theta=2*z*k*mu/(1+Hbar),
    x=b*w-a*theta/2=b*(w+Hbar*mu)/(1+Hbar), rho=x/beta,
    hcoef=theta/(2*k*mu), jcoef=a^2*theta/2,
    rstar=u*theta/2.

The positive Hbar solves

    (1-bt)*Hbar^2+(beta-2*bw)*Hbar-b*w^2/mu=0,

which follows by substituting theta(k) into F=0. Also

    0<rho<1, hcoef+jcoef=z,
    a*x-a*b*mu=hcoef, k*ell*mu^2+theta^2/(4*k*mu)
                                            =2*b*w*x-x^2.         (8)

The inequalities use mu<w and bw<=beta, with strictness supplied
by Hbar>0. The identities follow by expansion and F=0.

Full original feasibility itself forces v<2. Indeed the terminal
diagonal is n=||e4||_M^2=sum_(j<4)Lambda4j-s(s+2)>=0.
Every weight in the last row of (3) is at least v. Hence

    v*n<=s*(2C-(C+1)*v), so v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                       (9)

We now work on 0<v<2; v>=2 was already excluded by this original row.
Define the nonnegative correction

    qcorr=max(0,((1+v)*rstar-beta*b*v*c)
                         /(w+(1+v)*(1-rho))),
    rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho),
    X=beta*J+x*B.

The EXACT original-array remainder is

    eps:=k*R-D
       =(1+Hbar)*||X||_M^2
         -2*(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)*<E,X>_M+k*mu*u^2*m
         +cB*B0+c1*B1+c20*Lambda20+qcorr*Lambda02
         +c03*Lambda03+c04*Lambda04+c14*Lambda14+c23*Lambda23,      (10)

where

    cB=k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*rshift,
    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w), c20=beta*u-a*qcorr,
    c03=k*c03old-rshift+w*qcorr,
    c04=beta*b*v*c-(1+v)*rshift+w*qcorr,
    c14=beta*b*v*c, c23=k*c23old.

To check the identity, first combine (4) and (5). Its Gram part is

 beta^2*||J||_M^2+2*beta*b*w*<J,B>_M+k*ell*mu^2*Tcut
                                                 +k*mu*||Avec||_M^2.

Subtract 2*rshift*<E,B>_M+2*qcorr*<E,J+B>_M from that Gram part.
Restore them using (6) and (7); this gives precisely all costs in
(10). Since rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho), the subtraction differs
from 2*rstar*<E,B> by 2*qcorr*<E,X>/beta. F=0 and (8) turn the
uncorrected Gram into ||X||^2+k*mu*||uE-aX||^2. This proves (10).

Every displayed cost is nonnegative; cB,c1,c20,c03,c14,c23 are
STRICTLY positive. Here is the full sign and PSD check.

Let

    qmax=beta*u*(sqrt(k*mu*(1+Hbar))-a*k*mu).

Then

    0<=qcorr<qmax<beta*u/(2a).                                  (11)

If qcorr=0 this follows from Hbar>0. Otherwise put
tau=sqrt(Hbar/(1+Hbar)) in (0,1), Vprod=vbc, and
Sden=2+b+c+v. Direct substitution gives

    qcorr=(beta/a)*Vprod*(u*tau^2-a*beta)
                              /(Sden-Vprod+Vprod*tau^2),
    qmax=(beta*u/a)*tau/(1+tau).

The numerator of qcorr is positive in this case. The inequality
qcorr<qmax is equivalent to

    Vprod*(1+tau)*(u*tau-a*beta)<u*tau*Sden.

Its left side is smaller than 2*Vprod*u*tau. Moreover
2*Vprod<=2v<2+b+c+v=Sden, using bc<=1 and v<2. This proves the
strict inequality. The upper bound on qmax follows from
tau/(1+tau)<1/2.

Also rshift>0: when qcorr>0 its numerator is less than
(1+v)*rstar, while its denominator is strictly larger than
(1+v)*(1-rho). Thus qcorr<rstar/(1-rho).
At k=k0 the part of cB before a*rshift equals u*(1-bc)>=0;
it strictly increases with k. Hence cB>0. Since k>k0, c1>0.
Equation (11) gives c20>beta*u/2>0. Finally
rstar<k*u*mu*z=k*c03old and rshift<=rstar, proving c03>0.
The definition of qcorr gives c04>=0, and c14,c23>0.

Set

    alpha=(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)/(1+Hbar), Y=X-alpha*E,
    sigma=k*mu*u^2-(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+Hbar)>0.

The positivity of sigma is exactly qcorr<qmax. Thus (10) is

    eps=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)*||Y||_M^2+the nonnegative costs in (10).  (12)

In particular every full original certificate obeys D<=kR. This
necessity argument uses the actual original rows. Its comparison with
the complete older conditions is supplied by Sections1--9 of the
present joint text, not by a claim about an auxiliary optimizer.

## 4. The actual quadratic column and joint first-row budget

Define

    pquad=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,
    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,
    delta=u^2-2, W0=1+bc*(v-1).

Tp=e and Le=0. Telescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 gives

    ||pquad||_M^2=Q>=0,
    2<E,pquad>_M=u*(m-delta)
             +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).    (13)

For the row formula, let O0=sum_(j>0)Lambda0j and
I0=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40. Then m=1-a^2-aO0 and node0 flow
gives Lambda10=O0+2u-I0. With Te0=u e-a e0 and
Mp=Dmat pquad+T^T L^T pquad/2 one obtains

 pquad^T L e0=uO0+2au-a*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40),
 2<E,pquad>_M=2(1-a^2)-a*pquad^T L e0,

which gives (13). Both incoming weights 1-bc and W0 are nonnegative,
and 1-bc<=W0, including v<1.

By (12), eps>=sigma*m+c20*Lambda20+cB*B0. Define

    xi_cap=min(c20/(a^2*beta), cB/(a^2*W0)),

omitting the second term if W0=0. For every real xi with
xi<=xi_cap and xi<sigma/u,

    eps>=xi*u*delta-xi^2*Q/(sigma-xi*u).                         (14)

Subtract xi*u*delta using (13). All remaining multiplier coefficients
are nonnegative: for xi>=0 this is the definition of xi_cap and the
order of the incoming weights; for xi<0 the coefficients increase.
The remaining Gram expression after adding xi^2Q/(sigma-xi*u) is

    ||sqrt(sigma-xi*u)*E+xi*pquad/sqrt(sigma-xi*u)||_M^2>=0.

Thus this is the same ORIGINAL matrix and column, not independent
energy selections. Let Qplus=Q+u^2*delta. Sending xi to -infinity
shows Qplus>=0, and

    Qplus=a^2*(A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2).

That prerequisite is the old scalar-return condition and is not
counted as an independent improvement.

For completeness, when Q,Qplus>0 the free maximizing xi is
xi_free=(sigma/u)*(1-sqrt(Q/Qplus)). If xi_free<=xi_cap, (14)
has supremum

    Bcal=(sigma/u^2)*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.

Otherwise its supremum is

    Bcal=xi_cap*u*delta-xi_cap^2*Q/(sigma-xi_cap*u).

The latter case automatically has xi_cap<sigma/u. For Q>0,Qplus=0,
the supremum is sigma*Q/u^2. For Q=0,Qplus>=0 it is
u*delta*min(xi_cap,sigma/u), and it is zero when Q=delta=0.
These statements follow on putting y=sigma-xi*u>0: the objective is

 sigma*delta+2*sigma*Q/u^2-(delta+Q/u^2)*y-Q*sigma^2/(u^2*y).

The earlier first-row budget is also retained, on its stated domain
0<a<1 and a*eta<1, where

    eta=b/(1+w+v), nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,
    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2.

Its exact identity is

 Erow=nu*(m+a*(Lambda20+B0))+(1+eta)^2*M11
                 +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (15)

Here is a direct derivation. For orthonormal physical vectors U,V set
gfirst=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V and use saturated values on

 Xphys=(U+u*gfirst,U,-b*U,-(1+v)*eta*U,-eta*U),
 Gphys=(gfirst,U,-eta*U,-eta*U,-eta*U).

The nonzero residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-a*nu,
Q12=-(1+eta)*(b-eta), and Q13=-(1+eta)*v*eta. Every other finite
residual is zero. Use ||gfirst||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a and
<U,gfirst>+a||gfirst||^2=1-a*eta to check the target0 residuals.
The energy telescope gives Erow and the two gradient columns modulo
e are sqrt(nu)*E and (1+eta)*e1, proving (15).

In particular, for EVERY lambda>=0, (14) holds with its left side
eps+lambda*Erow, sigma replaced by sigma+lambda*nu, and cap replaced
by

 min((c20+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*beta),
                         (cB+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*W0)).              (16)

This follows by adding the TWO exact budgets before using (13), so
the same m and original incoming coefficients must pay for both.
It does not replace any of the older2948 conditions. For example,
putting Wrow=max(beta,W0), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow) in (13),(15),(2) gives

 u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).

For delta>0, v>1, ab<=1 and c(v-1)<=1, Krow=u and Q<1. These imply
Erow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and

    b<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)).

To see the latter equivalence, the direct factorization is

 u^2*(Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2)
    =((a+3)-(delta+u*C)*eta)*((a+3)+(u*C-delta)*eta).

The second factor is positive since C>A and u*A>delta. Substituting
eta=b/(C-A-b) in the positive first factor gives the displayed
bound. All its hypotheses remain.

## 5. A further matched full-column inequality near equality

In this section v>0 and all constants are the ones already defined.
Let

    dphys=a*b*c*v=(1+v)*z,
    nbar=s*(2C-(C+1)*v)/v>=0,
    rcoef=ell*mu^2+mu*hcoef^2>0,
    Lnorm=sqrt((u-a*alpha)^2/sigma+a^2/(1+Hbar)),
    Lterm=sqrt((alpha-beta)^2/sigma+1/(1+Hbar)),
    Kterm=(dphys/x)*Lterm*sqrt(nbar), Jfee=a/(2*x),
    Pcost=max(ell*mu*Hpre/cB,ell*mu*beta/c1,c03old/c03,1/k),
    Gcost=max(mu*Lnorm^2,Pcost).

All denominators are strictly positive. If nbar<0, the original
terminal budget (9) already excludes the word; it is never square-rooted.

Every full original array satisfies the following explicit scalar test.
Write y=sqrt(eps), which requires eps>=0, and set

 tau_max(y)=(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y
       +sqrt((sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)^2+4*jcoef*(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)))
                                                            /(2*jcoef),
 Psi(y)=rcoef*tau_max(y)^2
            +2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*tau_max(y)*y+Gcost*y^2.

Then

                              R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                 (17)

Here is the full derivation retaining the actual quadratic column.
The vector identity, checked in all five coordinates, is

    pquad=uE-aX-jcoef*B+dphys*e4+ab*e.

The original terminal rows give

    2<E,e4>_M=a*Lambda40-Lambda04,
    2<J,e4>_M=2<E,e4>_M+beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14.

Since X=Y+alpha E, they imply

 x<B,e4>_M=(alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M
                              -beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/2.

Taking the inner product of pquad with B therefore gives

 <B,pquad>_M=(u-a*alpha)<E,B>_M-a<Y,B>_M-jcoef*Tcut
       +(dphys/x)*((alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M)
       -dphys*beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/(2*x).       (18)

Let eps_G=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)||Y||_M^2 and let eps_C be the cost sum
in (12). Thus eps=eps_G+eps_C with both nonnegative. Weighted
Cauchy--Schwarz gives

 ||(u-a*alpha)E-aY||_M<=Lnorm*sqrt(eps_G),
 ||(alpha-beta)E+Y||_M<=Lterm*sqrt(eps_G).

Also ||e4||_M^2<=nbar by (9), and
c14=beta*dphys/a. Drop the negative Lambda40,Lambda41 terms in
(18), use Lambda14<=eps_C/c14, and use
<B,pquad>_M>=-sqrt(Tcut*Q). The result is

 jcoef*Tcut<=sqrt(Tcut*Q)
        +(Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut)+Kterm)*sqrt(eps_G)+Jfee*eps_C.          (19)

For the other direction (4) and Avec=(u-a*alpha)E-aY+hcoef*B give

 R<=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps_G)
                                    +mu*Lnorm^2*eps_G+Pcost*eps_C
  <=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps)+Gcost*eps.           (20)

The cost bound follows by comparing each cost in (4) to the cost of
the SAME multiplier in (12); their maximum ratio is precisely Pcost.
Thus costs and energies have not been chosen from different arrays.
Replacing eps_G,eps_C separately by eps in (19) gives, for
tcut=sqrt(Tcut)>=0,

 jcoef*tcut^2-(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)*tcut-(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)<=0.

Its nonnegative root is tau_max(y). The right side of (20) increases
with tcut>=0, which proves (17).

This supplies a quantified positive-gap exclusion test. Psi is
continuous and strictly increasing on [0,infinity), tends to infinity,
and

    Psi(0)=rcoef*Q/jcoef^2.

Consequently, if R>rcoef*Q/jcoef^2, there is a unique y0>0 with
Psi(y0)=R, and every full array must have

                           eps>=y0^2>0.                         (21)

Strict increase follows from the explicit nondecreasing positive
coefficients in tau_max and Gcost>0. This is an algebraic definition
and proof; no numerical root finding is used. In particular Q=0,R>0
forces a positive eps, even when delta=0 and the first-row bound
(14) alone is zero. This prices a further original cross column.
No uniform lower bound as v,ell,R,Q or other factors vanish is claimed.


# Appendix F. Reproduced all-value C82 construction and minimization

The following is reproduced proof text, not an accepted predecessor. Local equation labels and local notation restart within each historical transcript. Its scope is the one stated in the main inventory; no historical allocation or handoff instruction is renewed.

## 6. C82 triangle: exact nonzero price and a real interpolant

Here keep canonical h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, and let

    C=5+a+b+c+d, D=2+d, eta=bc/D,
    ell=max{0,c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2},
    t=a v-1, Htri(v)=(v+1)(a v-1).

Use parameters v>=b and z>=0 with ell<=z^2<=Htri(v). For nondegenerate
triangles take z>0; the boundary z=0 is legal by the same normal checks.
These inequalities imply t>=0. Define

    X_0=((1+a)v-1,z), G_0=(v,0),
    X_1=G_1=(-1,z),
    X_2=(b,-bz),     G_2=(b,0),
    X_3=(-bc,-bz),   G_3=(-eta,0),
    X_4=(-eta,-bz),  G_4=(-eta,0),
    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (22)

Every GD equation has the original four step lengths. For completeness,
K=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3} contains0. At target0 the residual normal is(t,z).
Its comparisons with G_1,G_2,G_3 and0 are, respectively,

    z^2-(v+1)t, (b-v)t, -(eta+v)t, -v t,

all nonpositive. At target1 the normal vanishes. At target2 the normal
is(0,-bz), so all horizontal-gradient sources give zero and source1
gives -bz^2. At target3 the normal is(-(1+d)eta,-bz). Its source1
residual is b*[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0; its horizontal-gradient
sources and optimum source are nonpositive. At target4 the normal is
(0,-bz), again giving zero for horizontal-gradient sources and -bz^2
for source1. Every residual into the optimum is zero. Thus all finite
and optimum pairs hold, and(16) gives one actual base function.

The finite zero-cost arcs4->1,4->2,4->3 show d_1=d_2=d_3=d_4=0.
The least cost into0 is

    Dprice=min{(v-b)t, (v+1)t-z^2}.                      (23)

The other incoming costs, (v+eta)t and v t from the optimum, are no
smaller than(v-b)t. Every path into0 has a final incoming arc, while
each minimizing source1 or2 is reached at cost zero. Therefore

    d_0=d_0^*=Dprice, all other d_i=d_i^*=0,
    Pflow=2(1+a)Dprice.                                 (24)

The complete fixed-geometry value optimum is attained by

    F'_0=F_0-Dprice, F'_i=F_i for1<=i<=4, F'_star=0.      (25)

All incoming residuals at0 increase by Dprice and stay nonpositive
by(23); all outgoing residuals from0 decrease. All other pairs are
unchanged. This is a direct check of all thirty ordered pairs including
the optimum, independently confirming the path calculation.

For the actual function use(8) with these values and the optimum.
Its affine constants beta_i are

    beta_0=-v t+Dprice<=-b t,
    beta_1=beta_2=beta_4=beta_star=0,
    beta_3=-(1+d)eta^2<0.

When t>0, the active normals in(9) are0 and(0,-bz). Therefore its
minimizer set is the segment joining these two points, and0 is the
closest minimizer to X_0, whose second coordinate z is nonnegative.
If t=0, z=0 and the initial normal also vanishes, with the same
closest minimizer0. Thus the true squared initial distance is

    ((1+a)v-1)^2+z^2.

The base telescoping energy and the exact optimized energy are

    Ebase(v,z)=((1+a)v-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2 eta^2,
    Eprice(v,z)=Ebase(v,z)-2(1+a)Dprice.                 (26)

In particular a generic claim that saturated projection values are
always rigid is FALSE. Formula(23) gives a strictly positive price
whenever v>b, t>0, and z^2<(v+1)t. This is a statement about a fixed
actual geometry, not yet about improvement over an optimized family.

## 7. Full C82 value-and-parameter minimum for0<a<1,0<b<=1

Keep c,d>0 arbitrary and ell as above. Put

    R=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell)]/(2a),
    v_cross=[1+ell/(b+1)]/a,
    v_stationary=a/(1-a),
    v_best=min{v_cross,max{R,v_stationary}},
    K=C^2 eta^2.                                       (27)

Then R>=1/a>b and R<=v_cross. The first assertion follows because
Htri has its nonnegative zero at1/a and is increasing thereafter. For
the second, Htri(R)=ell implies(b+1)(aR-1)<=ell.

The exact infimum of(26) over EVERY legal triangle parameter is

    Enew=(1-a^2)v_best^2-2(1+a)a v_best+1+2(1+a)
                +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.                 (28)

If ell>0 the minimizing triangle has z=sqrt(ell)>0 and v=v_best.
If ell=0 the same formula is attained by the displayed degenerate
legal data; alternatively it is the limit of nondegenerate triangles.

Here is the complete minimization. For fixed v>=R, the price switches
at z^2=(b+1)t. Below this value its coefficient of z^2 is1-b^2>=0;
above it the coefficient is1-b^2+2(1+a)>0. Therefore z^2=ell is always
a minimizer. For R<=v<=v_cross the second term in(23) is the minimum,
and(26) becomes

    J_left(v)=(1-a^2)v^2-2(1+a)a v+1+2(1+a)
                         +[2(1+a)+1-b^2]ell-K.         (29)

For v>=v_cross it becomes

    J_right(v)=(1-a^2)v^2+2(1+a)ab v+1-2(1+a)b
                              +(1-b^2)ell-K.           (30)

The two expressions agree at v_cross. J_right has strictly positive
derivative on that half-line. J_left is a strictly convex quadratic
with stationary point a/(1-a). Clipping that point to[R,v_cross]
proves(27)--(28), including both endpoint cases.

The original unpriced C82 minimum in this domain is

    Eold=((1+a)R-1)^2+(1-b^2)ell-K,                    (31)

since its energy increases in v and is minimized at z^2=ell. At v=R,
Htri(R)=ell makes Dprice=0, and J_left(R)=Eold.

Consequently value pricing adds EXACTLY NO information to this fully
optimized C82 condition whenever

    R>=a/(1-a).                                        (32)

In particular(32) holds throughout a^2+a<=1, because R>=1/a.
The positive root of a^2+a=1 is(sqrt(5)-1)/2. This includes the
first-step projection range39/100<=a<=41/100 from d3abee1b17da52da,
with its b range79/100<=b<=81/100. The C83 price was already zero there
by Section4, independently of this comparison.

When ell>0 and R<a/(1-a), one has v_cross>R and v_best>R, so the
optimized energy is strictly smaller. The precise positive reduction is

    Eold-Enew=(1-a^2)*[(R-v_stationary)^2
                                 -(v_best-v_stationary)^2]>0. (33)

Equivalently this strict-improvement regime is

    a^2+a>1,
    0<ell<(a^2+a-1)/(1-a)^2.                            (34)

This proves a strict improvement of the C82 FAMILY VALUE in its stated
domain. It does NOT prove that a word satisfying all the other current
necessary conditions is rejected by(28). In particular(34) alone is
not a surviving full-cone region and must not be advertised as one.

# Full-support completion and an endpoint-pairing obstruction

Status: complete joint research DRAFT, not verifier accepted. This is the
single recovery round authorized by TASK.md and master guidance
6c702a3eacae802d. It gives no threshold-reaching seed and no global
four-step nonexistence theorem. No numerical/model evaluation, word scan,
solver, independent replay, or fact_submit was used.

The accepted input is fact 04219554e41d9750, Section 10, with the
interpolation orientation justified there by fact 1252b8823bb5a290.
Every other assertion used below is derived in this document. Previous
drafts 666b5b9b14038a0f and b8c5ad58b9b356ca already contained the
constant kernel, quadratic calibration, and general real skew-completion
idea; those ideas are not claimed as new here. The new useful interfaces
are a nonnegative full-support center, all twelve affine directions about
it, the explicit released initial row, and a global neighborhood exclusion
for a stated two-parameter step construction.

## 1. Original formal space and the forced kernel

Let h=(h_0,h_1,h_2,h_3) have nonnegative entries, let s=sum_i h_i>0,
and set C=1+s. Indices of formal gradients run from 0 through 4.
Put P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, so P_0=0 and P_4=s. Let e be the column of
five ones, phi_i=1+P_i, and let T have diagonal 1, entry h_t at (i,t)
when t<i, and zero above its diagonal. Define

    omega=(-2h_0,-2h_1,-2h_2,-2h_3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_3(2-h_3),-s(s+2)).

For an array Lambda with zero diagonal let

    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    M=D+sym(LT),                 sym(A)=(A+A^T)/2.

The required full certificate is precisely

    Lambda_ij>=0 for all i!=j,  L^T e=omega,  M>=0 on R^5.       (1)

It certifies the primitive energy

    V_h=2 sum_(i<4) h_i(f_i-f_4)
        +sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_4||^2

through V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_4)>, where
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 and
x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t. This is the accepted identity, including
all original independent formal gradients at zero steps.

Since Te=e+P and sum_(i<4) h_i P_i=(s^2-sum h_i^2)/2,

    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2.

Thus every array with the stated flow has e^T M e=0. If M is PSD,
nonnegativity of (e+t z)^T M(e+t z) for both signs of t implies
Me=0. Its individual equations are

    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i),  i<4,
    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2s(s+1).                         (2)

For example, (T^T omega)_i=-2h_i+2h_i(P_i+h_i) at i<4, and direct
substitution gives (Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2. The terminal component
is -s(s+1)+(LP)_4/2. Consequently (2) is equivalent to Me=0 when
flow is already imposed.

## 2. An explicit nonnegative full-support flow/kernel center

Let k be the last index with h_k>0. Choose any mu strictly between
P_k and s; mu=(P_k+s)/2 is an exact choice. No P_i lies strictly
between P_k and s. Define

    w_i=2h_i/(mu-P_i) if h_i>0, and w_i=0 if h_i=0, i<4,
    w_4=2s/(s-mu),       W=sum_(i=0)^4 w_i,
    Lambda^c_ij=phi_i w_i w_j/W for i!=j, Lambda^c_ii=0.        (3)

All denominators in a nonzero w_i are positive. Thus this array is
nonnegative; if all four steps are positive, every one of its twenty
off-diagonal entries is strictly positive. It has initial-to-interior,
interior-to-initial, and both terminal incidences. Its positive row
factors are not proportional to step length: phi_i w_i/h_i equals
2(1+P_i)/(mu-P_i), which strictly increases with P_i.

To prove its identities, sum w_i(P_i-mu). Nonterminal positive indices
contribute -2h_i, and index 4 contributes 2s. The sum is zero, so

    sum_i w_i P_i=mu W,
    sum_i phi_i w_i=(1+mu)W.                                (4)

The outgoing mass minus incoming mass at node i equals

    phi_i w_i - w_i sum_j(phi_j w_j)/W
      =w_i(P_i-mu)=omega_i.

Its row moment is

    sum_j Lambda^c_ij(P_j-P_i)
      =phi_i w_i(mu-P_i),

which is 2h_i phi_i at i<4 and -2sC at i=4. Therefore this array
satisfies every flow and every constant-kernel equation. Write its
Laplacian and remainder as L_c and M_c.

This is deliberately NOT a PSD claim. A symbolic stress test is the
whole family h=(b,0,0,0), b>0. Formula (3) gives only
Lambda^c_04=2 and Lambda^c_40=2(1+b), independently of mu. Its full
five-dimensional remainder is

    M_c=(2-b^2)(e_0-e_4)(e_0-e_4)^T.                         (5)

Here e_i denotes a standard coordinate column. For b>sqrt(2) this is
negative on a formal direction, despite nonnegative coefficients,
correct flow, and the constant kernel. Zero-step gradients remain in
the matrix; they have zero rows in this particular array. The failure
also occurs with all twenty coefficients positive: for the symbolic
family h_i=b>2 for all i<4, (3) has that support, but
M_00=b(2-b)+(1-b)sum_(j>0)Lambda^c_0j<0. Thus neither positive
support nor the row-moment equations can replace PSD.

The canonical center has the special ratio
Lambda^c_ij/Lambda^c_ji=phi_i/phi_j when both weights are nonzero.
This is only a property of the center, not a restriction in the complete
family in the next section.

## 3. Every full-support completion: twelve unrestricted directions

Let E be the 5-by-4 matrix with columns e_i-e_4, i=0,1,2,3. Let F
be the 5-by-3 matrix with columns

    F_j=e_j-(1-P_j/s)e_0-(P_j/s)e_4, j=1,2,3.                 (6)

The columns of E form a basis of e-perp. The columns of F are
independent, annihilate both e and P under transpose, and form a basis
of span(e,P)-perp. Independence follows from their three middle
coordinates; e and P are independent because P_0=0<P_4=s.

Let X be ANY real 4-by-3 matrix and set

    L(X)=L_c+E X F^T,
    Lambda_ij(X)=Lambda^c_ij-(E X F^T)_ij for i!=j,
    Lambda_ii(X)=0.                                        (7)

Then L(X)e=0, L(X)^T e=omega and M(X)e=0. In particular, L(X)
is the row Laplacian of the displayed extracted array. The proof is
E^T e=0, F^T e=F^T P=0, followed by Te=e+P.

Conversely, let L be ANY real array Laplacian with the required flow
and Me=0, with no multiplier sign restriction yet. Put Delta=L-L_c.
Then Delta e=0, Delta^T e=0 and Delta P=0: the last follows from
0=(M-M_c)e=Delta P/2. Thus its columns lie in e-perp and its rows
lie in span(e,P)-perp. Expanding in these two bases gives uniquely
Delta=E X F^T. This proves exhaustiveness of (7), including arrays
with arbitrary initial remote edges, arbitrary reverse edges, and
arbitrary tail/bridge proportions. In particular, the special ratio
of the center is completely released.

The twenty sign constraints are the explicit affine inequalities

    (E X F^T)_ij <= phi_i w_i w_j/W, i!=j.                    (8)

When h_i>0 for every queried index, X=0 is a strict interior point
of this sign region in all twelve coordinate directions. It need
not satisfy PSD. For zero steps some signs can be on their boundary;
the representation still includes all formal coordinates and all arrays.

## 4. Exact full PSD test and the remaining three skew variables

Let B be the 5-by-4 matrix with columns e_0,...,e_3, and let

    K_c=B^T M_c B,      R=F^T T B.                            (9)

The leading four-dimensional block of M(X) is

    K(X)=K_c+sym(XR),                                       (10)

because B^T E=I. Since M(X)e=0, its entire matrix is

    M(X)=[K(X), -K(X)1; -1^T K(X), 1^T K(X)1].              (11)

Consequently M(X) is PSD on ALL of R^5 if and only if K(X) is PSD.
This is a congruence identity in the differences g_i-g_4, i<4, and
does not set any original formal gradient equal to another.

For explicit entries of K_c, put u_i=phi_i w_i, v_i=w_i/W and
t_i=(T^T v)_i=v_i+h_i sum_(j>i)v_j for i<4. Then L_c=diag(u)-u v^T,
and

    (K_c)_ii=h_i(2-h_i)+u_i(1-t_i),
    2(K_c)_ij=u_j h_i-u_i t_j-u_j t_i, i<j<4.                (12)

These give exact rational functions for rational h and rational mu.

Define p_0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. Then Tp=e. Let

    q=(p_0-p_4,p_1-p_4,p_2-p_4,p_3-p_4)^T,
    c_quad=1-C^2 p_4^2.                                    (13)

The vector q is nonzero: otherwise all p_i=1, forcing every h_i=0.
The kernel of F^T T is span(e,p), since F^T annihilates span(e,P)
and T^-1 P=e-p. Restriction to the subspace with final coordinate
zero shows that R has rank 3 and ker R=span(q).

Also, p^T M_c p=p^T Dp because Tp=e and L_c e=0. The identity
h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 telescopes and gives

    q^T K_c q=1-C^2 p_4^2=c_quad.                           (14)

Thus any proposed PSD K in (10) must obey q^T Kq=c_quad.
This condition is also sufficient for SOME real X, without signs.
Here are all such completions. Set H=K-K_c, so q^T Hq=0, and put

    S_0=[q(Hq)^T-(Hq)q^T]/(q^T q).

For an arbitrary skew-symmetric 4-by-4 matrix Z satisfying Zq=0,
let

    X=(H+S_0+Z)R^T(RR^T)^(-1).                             (15)

Indeed S_0 q=-Hq, so A=H+S_0+Z annihilates q. The matrix
R^T(RR^T)^(-1)R is the orthogonal projection onto q-perp;
therefore XR=A, and sym(XR)=H. Conversely any X with (10) has
XR-H skew-symmetric and its value on q is -Hq, giving exactly
S_0+Z. The possible Z form the three-dimensional skew space on
q-perp. No other freedom was omitted.

The COMPLETE feasibility problem at a fixed h is therefore: choose
K>=0 with q^T Kq=c_quad, choose the three skew variables of Z, use
(15), and satisfy ALL twenty inequalities (8). No second kernel is
imposed when c_quad>0. If c_quad=0, PSD forces Kq=0, and only then
the corresponding additional full-space kernel is legitimate.

Choosing a PSD K with the right scalar calibration alone does not solve
the twenty signs. For instance, when c_quad>=0 the formal choice
K=c_quad q q^T/(q^T q)^2 obeys the calibration, but (8) remains an
independent obligation. No universal nonnegative completion of that
rank-one choice is asserted. This is the precise remaining completion
problem, not a claimed certificate.

## 5. The released initial remote edges in Schur coordinates

This section assumes a=h_0>0 only to divide by a. The preceding
lossless coordinates do not make this assumption. Write
z_j=Lambda_0j and y_j=Lambda_j0 for j=1,...,4, and set h_4=0 here
as a notation convention. The initial row moment and flow are

    a z_1+sum_(j=2)^4 P_j z_j=2a,
    sum_(j=1)^4 y_j=2a+sum_(j=1)^4 z_j.                     (16)

The initial diagonal m=M_00 is exactly

    m=2-a^2+(a-1)/a sum_(j=2)^4 (P_j-a)z_j.                 (17)

To check it, first use M_00=a(2-a)+(1-a)sum_j z_j, then
sum_j z_j=2-a^(-1)sum_(j=2)^4(P_j-a)z_j from (16).
For j=2,3,4, the mixed entries are

    2 beta_j=2 M_0j=(a-1)y_j-z_j-h_j sum_(k>j)z_k.          (18)

In gradient differences u=g_0-g_1 and
v=(g_2-g_1,g_3-g_1,g_4-g_1), the full quadratic matrix is

    [m, beta^T; beta, K_tail],  K_tail=M_{[2,3,4],[2,3,4]}.

For m>0, its PSD condition is exactly
K_tail-beta beta^T/m>=0, by completing the square in u.
For m=0 it is exactly beta=0 and K_tail>=0; m<0 is impossible.

Formula (17) displays why keeping only Lambda_04 was a restriction:
Lambda_02 and Lambda_03 have distinct positive displacement weights
when a>1. Formula (18) displays the independent reverse-edge and
mixed-remainder freedoms. The one-chord cap 44fb89e3e8aa8c34 cannot
be extended to these variables without an additional argument. Even
the signs of their effect on the Schur complement are not fixed by
their effect on m alone.

## 6. Two necessary inequalities rederived on the full cone

Every certificate (1) with s>0 satisfies

    h_0,h_3 <= 2(s+1)/(s+2)=2C/(C+1)<2,                   (19)
    C |product_(i=0)^3(1-h_i)| <= 1.                       (20)

For the first endpoint, h_0<=1 is below the bound already. If
h_0>1, its row moment gives 2h_0<=s sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j,
whereas its nonnegative diagonal gives
h_0(2-h_0)>=(h_0-1)sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j. Combining and dividing by
h_0 yields s(2-h_0)>=2(h_0-1), which is (19).

For the last endpoint h_3=0 is immediate. If h_3>0, the terminal
row moment and terminal diagonal give

    2s(s+1)=sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)
             >=h_3 sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j
             >=h_3 s(s+2),

which proves its bound. No prefix or tail is assumed primitive.

Finally the telescope in (14) gives p^T M p=1-C^2 p_4^2 for every
array Laplacian, since Tp=e and Le=0. PSD makes it nonnegative.
Equivalently one may use the actual scalar function f(x)=x^2/2,
whose gradients are p_i and whose finite interpolation residuals all
vanish. This proves (20) without a saturation assumption.

## 7. Global obstruction to copying the endpoints into the interior

Define the endpoint-pairing distance of a schedule by

    d_pair(h)=min{
       max(|h_1-h_0|,|h_2-h_3|),
       max(|h_2-h_0|,|h_1-h_3|)}.                          (21)

This is an explicitly declared restriction on STEP DATA, not on the
twenty multipliers, their support, their skew part, or the rank of M.
The family d_pair=0 consists of the two arrangements (u,u,v,v) and
(u,v,u,v); nearby words need not have any exact symmetry or ordering.

Claim: there is no certificate (1) with C>=193/25 and
d_pair(h)<=1/20. This excludes the whole closed neighborhood,
including curved or distant changes to every coefficient.

Proof. Put delta=1/20. By (21) the two interior steps, in one of
the two orders, are h_0+epsilon and h_3+eta with
|epsilon|,|eta|<=delta. Put a=h_0-1, d=h_3-1, S=a+d. Then

    C=5+2S+epsilon+eta,
    S >= (C-5-2delta)/2 >=131/100.                         (22)

By (19) each endpoint is below 2. Thus C<9+2delta=91/10, and
another application of (19) gives

    a,d <=(C-1)/(C+1)<81/101<81/100=:r_bar.                (23)

It follows that a,d>S-r_bar>=1/2. In particular every step is
strictly greater than 1, so the absolute value in (20) equals the
positive product of all four step excesses. Also S<=2r_bar=81/50.
Writing p=ad and expanding (r_bar-a)(r_bar-d)>=0 yields

    p>=r_bar(S-r_bar)>=(81/100)(1/2)=81/200.               (24)

The four excesses have product

    a d (a+epsilon)(d+eta)
       >=p(a-delta)(d-delta)
       =p(p-delta S+delta^2)
       >=(81/200)(81/200-81/1000+1/400)
       =(81/200)(653/2000)
       =52893/400000 >13/100.                            (25)

Both factors in the penultimate lower bound are positive, so all
multiplications of bounds preserve their directions. Equations (20)
and (25) contradict each other because

    C product_i(h_i-1) > (193/25)(13/100)=2509/2500>1.

This proves the claim for the entire stated family, including all
nonnegative step boundary cases: (22)--(23) themselves force those
boundary cases out before (20) is used.

Consequently any actual threshold seed must have d_pair(h)>1/20.
Changing only full support or PSD rank while staying within that step
neighborhood cannot work. The exact result is a necessary quantitative
asymmetry, not a claim that asymmetry suffices. The remaining family
d_pair>1/20 is not excluded here.

## 8. Incorporating the missing clipping constraint with matched hypotheses

The exact interface above must still obey the legal-function constraints
identified in drafts 399341aaa6b09bdb and 7cd0bb4a795ee1df. Here is a
fresh derivation, rather than an import of either draft as a premise.

Suppose h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, so
C=5+a+b+c+d. For alpha>0 let

    f_alpha(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1,
               x^2/2 for -alpha<=x<=1,
               -alpha x-alpha^2/2 for x<=-alpha.

Its derivative is clamp(x,[-alpha,1]), which is continuous,
nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, and 0 is its unique minimizer. At every
point f_alpha(x)=x g-g^2/2. Telescoping the actual GD update gives

    V_h=x_0^2-(x_4+s g_4)^2.                              (26)

Indeed sum_i[2h_i x_i g_i-h_i^2 g_i^2]=x_0^2-x_4^2;
inserting the saturated values in V_h leaves -2s x_4g_4-s^2g_4^2.

Take x_0=P=2+a and alpha=b/(2+c). The actual points are

    x_1=1, x_2=-b, x_3=-alpha, x_4=d alpha,
    g_0=g_1=1, g_2=g_3=-alpha, g_4=min(d alpha,1).

If d alpha<=1, (26) is P^2-C^2 d^2 alpha^2. If d alpha>1,
x_4+s>C>P, and it is negative. Every full certificate therefore
requires

    C b d <= (2+a)(2+c).                                 (27)

This matches the hypotheses of 399341aaa6b09bdb exactly and uses
one actual function with all original pairs. In particular the old
rejected schedule interval is not proposed again in this document.

For completeness, on the stated SUBDOMAIN cd<1, the full lower-cap
family can be eliminated as follows. Set H=3+c+d and retain x_0=P.
Its energies are all nonnegative, for every alpha>0, if and only if

    Cb/H<=P,
    Cbd/(2+c)<=P,
    C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P,
    and if bc>=1, Cd<=P.                                 (28)

These conditions characterize this one scalar function family only.
No global necessity of cd<1 is imported from an unverified draft.
Whenever a construction lies in that subdomain, (28) applies in full.

Here is the complete regime proof. Set Y(alpha)=x_4+s g_4. Formula
(26) says |Y|<=P is exactly the nonnegative-energy condition. For
alpha>=b, x_3=bc and x_4=bc-(1+d)min(bc,1), independent of alpha.
If bc<=1, x_4=-bcd>-b. If bc>=1, b>=1/c>d and x_4>=-d>-b.
Hence its lower cap is inactive. The upper cap, if active, gives
Y=x_4+s>C>P; otherwise Y=Cx_4. In either event the condition is
exactly the third inequality in (28).

For 0<alpha<=b set A_cap=b/H and B_cap=b/(2+c); both lie in (0,b)
and A_cap<B_cap. Up to B_cap, g_3=-alpha and
x_4=-b+(2+c+d)alpha. Up to A_cap, g_4=-alpha and
Y=-b-(P+b)alpha decreases to -Cb/H. Between A_cap and B_cap,
x_4 increases, the lower cap is inactive, and Y increases, through
its quadratic and possible upper-cap branches. Its final value has
x_4=db/(2+c); feasibility there is precisely the second inequality.
The limit |Y(0)|=b is smaller than Cb/H because C>H.

Above B_cap, g_3=x_3=-b+(1+c)alpha until it reaches 1 or alpha=b.
Then x_4=-d x_3 decreases. The possible expressions for Y are
x_4+s, Cx_4, and x_4-s alpha, all decreasing in alpha, and they
agree at their clipping boundaries. Absolute maxima occur at the
ends of this interval. If bc<1, its upper end is b, already covered.
If bc>=1, the upper-cap transition is C_cap=(b+1)/(1+c)<=b.
Here C_cap>=1/c>d by cd<1, so x_4=g_4=-d and Y=-Cd.
This gives the fourth inequality. Above C_cap and up to b,
g_3=1 and x_4=-b+(1+c)alpha-(1+d) increases. Its lower cap stays
inactive, since x_4+alpha>0 at C_cap and increases; Y increases
on both remaining branches. Its endpoint values are already listed.
This covers every alpha and proves (28).

All strict violations of (27) or (28) yield negative energy from a
single specified convex smooth function. Such a word cannot be repaired
by any of the twelve completion directions in (7). Conversely, surviving
these tests still leaves the PSD/sign problem (8)--(15) open.

## 9. Outcome, failed shortcuts, and exact next freedom

The original requested threshold certificate has not been constructed.
The concrete outcome is a whole-family obstruction (21)--(25), plus a
lossless full-support construction interface for schedules outside it.
The smallest parameter restriction removed by the obstruction is explicit:
at least one interior-to-endpoint discrepancy must exceed 1/20 under
each possible matching. The coefficient problem after that release is
exactly (8)--(15), or its full initial-row Schur form (16)--(18).

Two tempting shortcuts were checked symbolically and rejected. Taking
the dense positive center already solves flow and the constant kernel,
but (5) proves that it does not solve PSD in general. Choosing a PSD
remainder with only the quadratic calibration solves the real linear
representation problem, but it leaves all twenty affine signs in (8),
and all actual clipping witnesses still apply. No argument here excludes
curved or distant gains outside the explicitly defined paired family.

The short literature check found no directly applicable theorem for the
new positive center. The exact local Zhang--Jiang source (arXiv:2410.12395,
Definition 7, Proposition 8, Theorem 11 and its proof) was read to check
the primitive convention. No new external theorem is used above; the
graph-weight analogy is elementary algebra inside the already accepted
finite certificate interface.

Self-audit: justification is explicit coefficient algebra and exact
inequalities, with special attention to the off-diagonal sign convention,
zero steps, the rank of R, and absence of an unjustified second kernel.
All mathematical conclusions remain unverified drafts. No fact_id is
claimed for them, and the original minimax target remains open.

# Exact initial-chord coefficient family and its capacity obstruction

Status: **UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate**, max4, cumulative round24.
The current TASK authorizes symbolic research but no new fact_submit,
numerical discovery, replay, installation, or publication. No new fact is
claimed. The accepted input is04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11, for the
original full primitive matrix and ConPP identities. Every new assertion
used below is derived in this document. Earlier drafts are not premises.

The result concerns a specified family of full coefficient arrays. It is
not a characterization of the full four-step cone. Within this family the
tail's entire symmetric PSD remainder and every admissible skew completion
are free; neither its rank nor its incoming-terminal signs are frozen.
The initial-to-terminal coefficient is also free. The family has an exact
nonzero-chord rational example, but its four-step capacity cannot exceed
the exact non-dyadic ConPP baseline, which is below193/25.

## 1. Original formal identity and the kernel used here

For a nonnegative word h of length n, put s=sum h_i, C=1+s,
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, and retain every independent formal gradient g_0,...,g_n.
Use the original convention

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                             -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

For any REAL off-diagonal array lambda, not necessarily nonnegative, let
L=diag(lambda*e)-lambda, where e is the all-ones column. Let T_ii=1,
T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i. Define

 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<n,-s(s+2)).

The value cancellation and full gradient remainder are exactly

 L^T e=omega,
 M=D_prim+sym(LT),
 V_h+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>.                 (1)

Here sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2. These formulas are the accepted full identity,
which is linear in the array and hence also valid for real signed arrays.
The position contribution is sym(L(T-I)), and the gradient-difference
contribution is sym(L)-diag(omega)/2; this also checks (1) directly.

Flow alone gives e^T M e=0. Indeed

 e^T D_prim e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
 e^T LT e=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2,

using Te=(1+P_i)_i and 2sum h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2.
If M is PSD, then Me=0: nonnegativity of (e+t v)^T M(e+t v)
for both signs of sufficiently small t forces v^T Me=0 for every v.
The individual nonterminal rows are therefore

 sum_j lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i),  i<n.              (2)

The terminal row is sum_(j<n)lambda_nj(s-P_j)=2s(s+1).
No second kernel is assumed. Once Me=0, the quadratic is invariant under
subtracting the same vector from every formal gradient.

For a nonnegative array and h_0=a>0, (2) also proves a<2 whenever s>0.
The first outgoing mass is positive, because sum_j lambda_0j P_j=2a.
The first diagonal is a(2-a)+(1-a)sum_j lambda_0j. It is strictly negative
if a>=2, contradicting PSD. We will need only this weak endpoint bound.

## 2. A freely variable signed tail and the full coefficient construction

Let a>0, x>=0, and let b=(b_0,...,b_(m-1)) be any nonnegative tail.
Set

 A=1+a, D=1+sum b_i, C=A+D+x,
 R=D+x, H=A+x, delta=AD-Cx,
 k=C^2/D^2, t=2CH/(DR), gamma=2k-2-t,
 sigma=H*delta/(D^2 R).                                  (3)

Thus the actual parent word is h=(a,1+x,b), its capacity is C, and
C-1=a+R. Indices0,1 are the initial query and bridge query. Tail local
indices i=0,...,m correspond to original indices i+2; write z=m+2 for
the original terminal index. Define weights w_i=b_i for i<m and w_m=1,
so sum_i w_i=D.

Take ANY real tail array ell that has value cancellation for V_b and a
FULL PSD tail remainder M_b. Require ell_ij>=0 on every edge other than
the incoming-terminal edges i<m,j=m. Those incoming entries can be
negative, subject only to the actual parent signs below. All formal tail
gradients, including at zero steps, remain independent. By Section1,
M_b has the constant-gradient kernel.

Choose a parameter Z>=0 and set

 mu=2-a^2+(a-1)R Z/a.

Define the following COMPLETE parent array; all entries not listed are0:

 lambda_01 = 2-(C-1)Z/a,
 lambda_10 = 2A-R Z/a,
 lambda_0z = Z,
 lambda_1z = 2AH/R-Z,
 lambda_(i+2,1) = t w_i,                     0<=i<=m,
 lambda_(i+2,j+2) = k ell_ij
                   +gamma*b_i*1_(i<m,j=m),  i!=j.        (4)

All indices in (4) are original indices. If m=0, the sole tail gradient
is the terminal one, and there are no within-tail entries. In the actual
four-step application m=2 and there are five formal gradients.

The exact sign conditions are

 2-(C-1)Z/a>=0,  2A-R Z/a>=0,  2AH/R-Z>=0,
 k ell_(i,m)+gamma b_i>=0 for every i<m,                 (5)

together with Z>=0 and the nonnegative nonterminal tail entries already
specified. Every remaining listed coefficient is automatically
nonnegative, because k,t>0 and

 gamma=2H[D^2+(C+D)x]/(D^2 R)>=0.

In particular (5) retains the exact lower bound on each signed incoming
tail coefficient, instead of replacing it by ordinary tail primitivity.

Write

 U=g_0-g_1,
 J=sum_(i<m)b_i g_(i+2)+g_z-Dg_1.

The full formal identity for (4) is

 V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij
   = k<M_b,Gram(g_2,...,g_z)>
       +mu||U||^2-Z<U,J>+sigma||J||^2.                  (6)

Consequently, under the stated tail hypotheses and (5), (4) is a full
nonnegative primitive certificate IF AND ONLY IF

             [ mu,   -Z/2 ]
             [ -Z/2, sigma ] >=0.                     (7)

This is equivalent to mu>=0, sigma>=0 and Z^2<=4mu*sigma.
The equivalence is on the whole original formal gradient space.

### Complete algebraic check of (6)

The relations needed for flow and row moments are

 2k=2+t+gamma,
 gamma D-tx=2H,
 tD=2CH/R,
 C=A+R=D+H.                                            (8)

At node0, outgoing minus incoming is
2-(C-1)Z/a+Z-(2A-RZ/a)=-2a.
At node1 it is 2a+Z+(2AH/R-Z)-tD=-2(1+x).
At a nonterminal tail node it is
(-2k+gamma+t)b_i=-2b_i.
The terminal flow then follows by summing all flows.

For the kernel, node0 has moment
a lambda_01+(C-1)lambda_0z=2a.
Node1 has moment -a lambda_10+R lambda_1z=2A(1+x).
For a tail index with local prefix q_i=sum_(j<i)b_j, its within-tail
moment plus its edge to node1 is

 2k b_i(1+q_i)+gamma b_i(D-1-q_i)
                       -t b_i(1+x+q_i)
   =2b_i(1+H+q_i),

by (8). This is (2) at its original prefix P_(i+2)=H+q_i.
Thus every nonterminal row of Me vanishes. Flow gives e^TMe=0, so the
terminal row vanishes too. This calculation does not assume (7).

It now suffices to compute after setting g_1=0. Let
G=sum_(i<m)b_i g_(i+2) and q=g_z. The coefficient of ||g_0||^2 is

 a(2-a)+(1-a)(lambda_01+Z)=mu,

and the only cross term involving g_0 and tail gradients is
-Z<g_0,G+q>. For the tail-only part, subtract its supplied identity
k M_b. The gamma sum of incoming-tail Q and the t sum of tail-to-bridge
Q cancel all coefficients of sum b_i(1-b_i)||g_(i+2)||^2, by
1-k+(gamma+t)/2=0. Their remaining coefficients of ||G||^2 and
<G,q> are (t-gamma)/2 and t-gamma. The remaining ||q||^2 coefficient is

 -(C-1)C+k(D-1)D
      +[t+(2AH/R-Z)+Z+gamma(D-1)]/2
   =(t-gamma)/2.

For this equality, use kD^2=C^2 and 2AH/R=tD-2H.
Finally

 (t-gamma)/2=t-(k-1)=H(AD-Cx)/(D^2R)=sigma.

This proves (6) with g_1=0. The already checked constant kernel restores
the displayed U,J form for arbitrary g_1.

Sufficiency of (7) follows from (6) and M_b PSD. For necessity, set every
tail gradient equal to one freely chosen vector v. Its tail quadratic is
zero. Then J=D(v-g_1) and U=g_0-g_1 can be varied independently, so full
PSD forces precisely (7). This argument does not delete a formal
coordinate or prescribe equality in the statement of the certificate.

## 3. A nonzero-chord rational four-step certificate

For a=1, x=0 and b=(1,1), take the tail array

 ell_01=2, ell_10=4, ell_12=8, ell_21=12,

with other entries0. Expanding (1) gives
M_b=||g_2-g_3||^2+4||g_3-g_4||^2 in original indices.
Choose Z=1/2. Then C=5, k=25/9, t=20/9, gamma=4/3,
mu=1 and sigma=4/9. The complete nonzero parent array is

 10:5/2, 04:1/2, 14:13/6,
 21:20/9, 31:20/9, 41:20/9,
 23:50/9, 32:100/9, 24:4/3, 34:212/9, 43:100/3.         (9)

Its word is (1,1,1,1), and its full remainder is exactly

 (25/9)[||g_2-g_3||^2+4||g_3-g_4||^2]
       +||U-J/4||^2+(55/144)||J||^2,
 U=g_0-g_1, J=g_2+g_3+g_4-3g_1.                        (10)

Thus the family does allow a nonzero new chord and a changed positive
remainder. Equations(9)--(10) follow symbolically from (3)--(6); no model
evaluation or arithmetic replay was run. Its constant-gradient kernel is
the only kernel, since the four displayed linear forms are independent
modulo a common gradient. This example is a calibration, not a competitive
four-step seed.

## 4. Two-step signed tails cannot acquire extra capacity

This section rederives the capacity consequence of the older short-tail
re-selection draft f3d96c901566e96b. It is NOT a new claim of priority and
does not use that unverified draft as a premise.

Put r=sqrt(2), lambda=1+r, and

                 D2=Omega(1,lambda).

Suppose a tail consists of u,v>=0 and has a real value-canceling full PSD
identity, with every coefficient nonnegative except possibly ell_02 and
ell_12. There is no lower bound on these two signed entries in this
section. Then

                       1+u+v<=D2.                      (11)

First let u,v>0, S=u+v and D=1+S. Let p=ell_01>=0 and q=ell_10>=0.
The two nonterminal kernel rows from (2) force

 ell_02=u(2-p)/S,
 ell_12=2(1+u)+q u/v>0.                                (12)

The first diagonal is

 M_00=u(2-u)+(1-u)(v p+2u)/S.                           (13)

If u>r, then u>1 and (13) is decreasing in p. At p=2 it is2-u^2<0.
PSD therefore forces p<2, so (12) makes ell_02>0 also. The entire
supplied tail array is then nonnegative.

Irrespective of the signs in (12), terminal outgoing coefficients are
nonnegative. Its kernel moment and diagonal give

 S ell_20+v ell_21=2S(S+1),
 ell_20+ell_21>=S(S+2).

Since S>=v, these imply v<=2D/(D+1). If u<=r, substitute
v=D-(1+u) to obtain

 D^2-(2+u)D-(1+u)<=0,
 D<=Omega(1,1+u)<=Omega(1,lambda)=D2.

If u>r, the array has already been proved nonnegative. Put
O=ell_01+ell_02. Its first moment gives 2u<=S O, and its first diagonal
gives (u-1)O<=u(2-u). Therefore 2(u-1)<=S(2-u), equivalently
u<=2D/(D+1). If v<=r, that bound
gives D<=Omega(1,1+v)<=D2.

It remains to treat u>r and v>r. On f(x)=x^2/2 with initial point1,
the three gradients are1,1-u,(1-u)(1-v), and all finite Q vanish.
The identity sum h_i(2-h_i)g_i^2=1-g_2^2 gives
V_b=1-D^2(1-u)^2(1-v)^2. Thus (1) and PSD give

                 D (u-1)(v-1)<=1.                     (14)

On the other hand (u-r)(v-r)>0 implies

 (u-1)(v-1)>(r-1)(D-2-r).

The positive root D2 satisfies D2^2-(2+r)D2-(1+r)=0, hence
(r-1)D2(D2-2-r)=1. The function (r-1)D(D-2-r) is strictly increasing
for D>=D2. Thus D>=D2 contradicts (14). This proves (11) for positive
steps, without assuming the tail is already primitive.

For u=0,v>0, the zero-step row forces ell_02=0. The terminal moment
gives ell_20+ell_21=2(v+1), so its diagonal is2-v^2 and v<=r.
For v=0,u>0, the row at index1 forces ell_10=0, and the row at index0
forces ell_01+ell_02=2. The first diagonal is2-u^2, so u<=r.
For u=v=0 the capacity is1. Thus all zero-step boundaries are included
without identifying or deleting formal gradients.

## 5. An exact obstruction for the ENTIRE parameterized family

Set

             C0=Omega(lambda,D2),
             x0=C0-lambda-D2>0.                        (15)

Every four-step certificate from (3)--(7), with ANY two-step signed tail
permitted there, has

                         C<=C0.                        (16)

This is global in the family's parameters a,x,Z and the entire tail
array. It includes curved or distant variations; it is not a first-order
normal or a fixed-remainder assertion.

By (11), D<=D2. By the original nonnegative parent signs and PSD, a<2.
Suppose first a<=r. Since H,D,R>0, sigma>=0 implies delta>=0, that is
Cx<=AD. As x=C-A-D>=0, this is equivalent to

 C<=Omega(A,D)<=Omega(lambda,D2)=C0.

Now suppose r<a<2 and C>=C0. Put eps=a^2-2>0 and K=(a-1)R/a>0,
so mu=-eps+K Z. Condition (7) has sigma>0; if sigma=0, it would force
Z=0 and mu=-eps<0. Its determinant inequality gives

 (Z-2sigma K)^2<=4sigma(sigma K^2-eps),
                         sigma K^2>=eps.               (17)

The exact simplification is

 HR=AD+Cx,
 sigma K^2=(a-1)^2[(AD)^2-(Cx)^2]/(a^2D^2),
 (a-1)^2 A^2-a^2(a^2-2)=1.

Consequently (17) forces the especially small algebraic obstruction

                        C(a-1)x<=D.                    (18)

For fixed a and x=C-A-D>=0, the expression
C(a-1)(C-A-D)/D increases with C and decreases with D. The former follows
from derivative (a-1)(2C-A-D)/D>0, and the latter follows by writing it
as (a-1)[C(C-A)/D-C]. Therefore C>=C0,D<=D2 imply

 C(a-1)x/D >= (C0/D2)(a-1)(r+x0-a).

The source relation C0 x0=lambda D2 and lambda(r-1)=1 gives

 (C0/D2)(a-1)(r+x0-a)-1
       =(C0/D2)(a-r)(1+x0-a).                          (19)

We have x0>1. Indeed D2>3, since its defining polynomial at3 is2-4r<0;
thus (lambda-1)(D2-1)=r(D2-1)>2. The polynomial
x(lambda+D2+x)-lambda D2 is strictly increasing for x>=0 and negative
at1, proving x0>1. Since r<a<2<1+x0, (19) is strictly positive.
This contradicts (18). It excludes even equality C=C0 in the a>r case
and completes the proof of (16).

The contradiction in (18)--(19) already ignores the additional upper
bounds on Z in (5). Thus no adjustment of those nonnegative coefficients,
no tail skew re-selection, and no increase of the tail PSD rank can repair
this family above C0.

For an explicit rational comparison, r<99/70 gives lambda<169/70.
At D=201/50, the polynomial D^2-(1+lambda)D-lambda is strictly greater
than362/17500>0, hence D2<201/50. At C=77/10,

 C^2-(169/70+201/50)C-(169/70)(201/50)=71/1750>0.

Monotonicity of Omega yields

                       C0<77/10<193/25.                 (20)

This proves the family cannot meet the required s>=168/25.

## 6. Exact length-four ConPP calibration at the cap

Let y=D2-1-lambda, so y>0 and y D2=lambda. Take the actual word

                    h0=(r,1+x0,1+y,r).                 (21)

It has capacity C0. Put k2=(D2/lambda)^2, t2=k2-1,
k4=(C0/D2)^2, and t4=k4-1. Its complete nonzero array is

 01:2, 10:2lambda,
 21:t4(1+y), 31:t4 r, 41:t4, 14:t4 x0,
 32:k4 t2 r, 42:k4 t2,
 24:k4 t2 y+t4(1+y),
 34:k4(2k2+t2 r)+t4 r,
 43:2lambda k4 k2.                                     (22)

Every entry is nonnegative. The inner word (1+y,r) is the ConPP join
of the empty word and the one-step r word; the outer word joins (r)
to that inner word. The accepted identity04219554e41d9750, Section11,
gives (22) with zero FULL remainder: the empty remainder and the
one-step remainder (2-r^2)||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2 both vanish.

It also calibrates the new formulas directly: take a=r, x=x0,
D=D2, Z=0. Then delta=0, sigma=mu=0, t=gamma=k4-1 and
lambda_1z=x0 t4. The free signed tail is the displayed ordinary
zero-remainder tail. Thus (4) reproduces (22) in its original five
coordinates, rather than merely matching a scalar objective value.

No quadratic kernel was imposed during the construction or obstruction.
At this exact source the zero full matrix and its kernels are consequences
of the displayed certificate. The rational example(9) has a different
remainder and only the constant kernel.

## 7. Restriction, counterexample to a global reading, and next interface

The declared family omits all tail-to-initial edges, all initial-to-tail
edges except the terminal chord, and all bridge-to-tail edges except its
terminal edge. It also forces the tail-to-bridge entries to be proportional
to (b_0,...,b_(m-1),1). These are construction restrictions, NOT consequences
of the full interface in04219554e41d9750.

For a concrete counterexample to the claim that every full certificate
has the proportionality restriction, consider the same unit word
(1,1,1,1). Set, for i=0,1,2,3,

 lambda_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,
 lambda_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2),

with all other entries0. Direct flow gives (-2,-2,-2,-2,8), and expansion
of (1) gives sum_(i=0)^3 (i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2. In its cut after
index1, the outgoing tail-to-bridge entries are (12,0,0), not proportional
to (1,1,1). This array lies outside the family even though the same
word also has the different inside-family array(9). Thus this example
refutes an array-level claim only; it does not prove that the restricted
family fails to represent the existence of a certificate for some word.

The smallest obstruction to the attempted capacity gain is(18), together
with the short-tail cap and exact source factorization(19). Increasing
the PSD rank within this family cannot change it. A subsequent construction
must release an actual restriction in Section7, for example independent
tail-to-bridge coefficients, initial chords to nonterminal tail samples,
or tail-to-initial coefficients. Such a construction must again check
every actual sign and the entire original matrix. No useful member of
that enlarged family, and no separator for the unrestricted full cone,
is supplied here.

The original four-step seed and the original asymptotic target remain
open. This document adds no verifier-accepted fact and uses no numerical
search, solver, finite replay, or new verification allocation.
