# Appendix B: complete actual C81 triangle construction and energy

# C81: a four-step triangle with an interior-edge query

Status: complete new joint UNVERIFIED construction. No verifier call is
allocated. This is analytical continuation, cumulative round19/19, of
the CLOSED C79 discovery allocation. No optimization, candidate sweep,
or exact numerical replay is performed here. The only fixed schedule
specialized below is the already saved record100.

The accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, supplies the original
primitive energy and its full certificate identity. All new geometry and
energy calculations below are proved directly rather than imported from
the unverified C79/C80 drafts.

## 1. General actual function and four-step trajectory

Let h=(a,b,c,d), with a>1, b>1, c>1, d>=0. Define

 B=b-1, C=c-1, D=d+1, s=a+b+c+d, A=1+s.

Choose u>0 and z>0 satisfying

 u>=B,
 z^2 <= (u+1)((a-1)u-1),
 z^2 >= C d(D-BC)/D^2.                                  (1)

The second inequality implies (a-1)u-1>0. The third imposes
no additional restriction if its right side is negative. Put

 p=(u,0), q=(-1,z), r=(-BC/D,0), K=conv{p,q,r},
 F(x)=max_(g in K) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                      (2)

This nondegenerate compact triangle contains0 in the relative interior
of its horizontal base. Completing the square shows that the unique
maximizer in (2) is the Euclidean projection P_K(x).

For completeness, if p_x=P_K(x), p_y=P_K(y), the two projection
normal inequalities give

 ||p_x-p_y||^2 <= <p_x-p_y,x-y>.

Hence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing formula gives

 0<=F(x+t)-F(x)-<p_x,t>
       <=<P_K(x+t)-p_x,t><=||t||^2,

so F is differentiable with gradient P_K; it is convex as a supremum
of affine functions. Since0 belongs to K, F>=0 and F(0)=0. Moreover
P_K(x)=0 exactly when x is in the normal cone of K at0. Pairing x
with both signs of the horizontal base forces its first coordinate
to vanish; pairing it with q then forces its second coordinate<=0.
Thus argmin F={(0,t):t<=0}.

The proposed ORIGINAL iterates and gradients are

 x0=(a u-1,z),       g0=(u,0),
 x1=(-1,z),         g1=(-1,z),
 x2=(B,-Bz),        g2=(B,0),
 x3=(-BC,-Bz),      g3=(-BC/D,0),
 x4=(-BC/D,-Bz),    g4=(-BC/D,0).                        (3)

The four equations x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i follow by subtraction.
The initial point has positive second coordinate, so its closest
minimizer is0 and its squared distance is(a u-1)^2+z^2.

We check every required projection normal, including both terminal
orientations implicitly through the single actual function.

At x0, the residual y0=x0-g0=((a-1)u-1,z) has

 <q-p,y0>=z^2-(u+1)((a-1)u-1)<=0,
 <r-p,y0>=(-BC/D-u)((a-1)u-1)<0.

At x1 the residual is0. At x2, g2 is on the base because
-BC/D<0<B<=u, and y2=x2-g2=(0,-Bz) has zero pairing with
both base directions and pairing-Bz^2<0 with q-g2.

At x3 the residual is y3=(-BCd/D,-Bz). Its base comparison
with p-r is nonpositive, and

 <q-r,y3>=B{C d(D-BC)/D^2-z^2}<=0.

At x4 the residual y4=(0,-Bz) has zero pairing with the base
and pairing-Bz^2<0 with q-r. These are all vertices of K,
so (3) gives the actual gradient of the same F at every query.

For all five indices define f_i=F(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
Every original ordered interpolation residual has the exact form

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
     =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                              (4)

This follows from the proved projection normals. The optimum sample0
also obeys all ordered comparisons. Thus no guessed rank or deleted
gradient is needed to certify legality.

## 2. Exact intrinsic energy and a necessary family of inequalities

Use the energy from accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10:

 V_h=2sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f4)
       +sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2.

The identity

 ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2
     =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2

follows from (3) and f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
Summing it gives

 V_h=||x0||^2-||x4||^2-2s f4-s(s+1)||g4||^2.

Here f4=||g4||^2/2, ||x4||^2=(BC/D)^2+B^2z^2, and therefore

 V_h=(a u-1)^2+(1-B^2)z^2-A^2(BC/D)^2.                 (5)

Consequently the original full primitive cone must satisfy

 (a u-1)^2+(1-B^2)z^2 >= A^2(BC/D)^2                  (6)

for EVERY u,z obeying(1). Indeed its accepted exact identity is
V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>, where Lambda_ij>=0,
the flow holds, and M is PSD in all five original gradients.
The right side is nonnegative and every Q_ij<=0, so it implies V_h>=0.
A negative value in(5) contradicts this identity directly.

This is a necessary family, not a sufficient condition. The imposed
a,b,c>1 is a scope assumption of the witness and is NOT a lossless
restriction of the full four-step search domain.




# Appendix C: complete C83 curvature repair with every original residual

# C83: exact all-curvature repair at a cut

Status: complete joint UNVERIFIED analytical candidate. No new schedule,
optimizer/model evaluation, numerical replay, installation or verifier
call. This strengthens the shared unit-curvature cut/quad idea by using
every curvature kappa in(0,1], retaining its strictly negative original
interpolation residuals, and deriving the exact maximal repair weight.
The unit-curvature shared draft is motivation only; everything needed
is rederived here. Accepted facts used:1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation)
and04219554e41d9750 Section10 (full primitive certificate identity).

## 1. Data and statement of the necessary condition

Let h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) be any finite nonnegative word, let
s=sum h_i,A=1+s, and select an index j with h_j>1. Define

 P=sum_(i<j)h_i, R=sum_(i>j)h_i, H=P+h_j=s-R,
 eta=(h_j-1)/(1+R)>0, C0=eta(1+eta),
 V0=(1+P)^2-A^2 eta^2.

For kappa in(0,1] let

 p_0=1, p_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa h_t) for0<=i<=j+1,
 E=1+kappa R, q=kappa p_(j+1)/E,
 t_kappa=(1-kappa)/(2kappa).

If R>0 put

 beta=max{0, max_(0<=i<=j)
       [q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R]}.

If R=0 put beta=0. Finally define

 V_kappa=
   kappa sum_(i=0)^j h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
   -H[s+R+1+1/kappa]q^2.                               (1)

Every full primitive certificate for the ORIGINAL word necessarily
satisfies, for every such j and every0<kappa<=1,

             beta V0+C0 V_kappa >=0.                    (2)

For beta=0 the stronger explicit interpretation is V_kappa>=0,
which is equivalent to(2) because C0>0. No other step is assumed
positive, above1, symmetric, ordered or part of a primitive prefix.

## 2. The legal scalar cut coordinate

Use the scalar convex function with derivative clip(x,-eta,1),
minimum0 at0, and initial point1+P. Put
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and R_i=sum_(t>=i)h_t. Its actual data are

 x_i^0=1+P-P_i,    g_i^0=1,             i<=j,
 x_i^0=-eta(1+R_i), g_i^0=-eta,         i>j,
 f_i^0=x_i^0 g_i^0-(g_i^0)^2/2.

The selected step sends1 to1-h_j=-eta(1+R), and the remaining
updates keep the points on the negative constant-gradient segment,
ending at-eta. All zero steps retain their original sample indices.
This verifies the full GD chronology on one actual function.

For this coordinate the ordered residual is

 Q_ik^0=(g_i^0-g_k^0)(x_k^0-g_k^0).

It is0 inside either block. From the prefix to a suffix target k>j,
it is exactly-C0 R_k. In the opposite direction it is
-(1+eta)(P-P_k)<=0. All optimum comparisons are nonpositive.
The norm-telescoping identity, or direct substitution, gives its
primitive energy V0 exactly as defined above.

## 3. A quadratic prefix and constant-gradient suffix

Define a second set of scalar data, not yet claimed to be legal:

 x_i^1=p_i, g_i^1=kappa p_i, f_i^1=kappa p_i^2/2,       i<=j;
 x_i^1=q(1/kappa+R_i), g_i^1=q,
 f_i^1=q x_i^1-q^2/(2kappa),                           i>j.

The point after the selected step is p_(j+1)=q(1/kappa+R),
so these data follow every original GD update and end at q/kappa.
Include the optimum sample (x_star^1,g_star^1,f_star^1)=(0,0,0).

Write Q_ik^1=f_i^1-f_k^1+g_i^1(x_k^1-x_i^1)
                  +(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2/2.
Direct substitution gives the COMPLETE list of residual types:

 * i,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1-g_k^1)^2<=0.
 * i,k>j: Q_ik^1=0.
 * i>j,k<=j:
   Q_ik^1=-t_kappa(q-kappa p_k)^2<=0.
 * i<=j,k>j:
   Q_ik^1=R_k q(kappa p_i-q)
                    -t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2.
 * Q_(i,star)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2<=0 for all i.
 * Q_(star,i)^1=-t_kappa(g_i^1)^2 for i<=j, and
   Q_(star,i)^1=-(R_i+t_kappa)q^2 for i>j.

Thus only the fourth type can be positive. If R_k=0 it is
nonpositive. For R_k>0, its quotient by R_k is

 q(kappa p_i-q)-t_kappa(kappa p_i-q)^2/R_k,

which is nondecreasing as R_k increases. Since0<R_k<=R,
its largest possible value is at k=j+1, whose R_k equals R.
This proves that beta is the exact largest nonnegative ratio of
a possibly bad residual to its corresponding suffix mass.

In particular beta=0 makes ALL auxiliary data, including optimum
comparisons, legal. If R=0 there are no positive suffix masses,
so the convention beta=0 has exactly this same meaning.

## 4. Orthogonal repair gives an actual globally legal function

If beta>0 choose epsilon=C0/beta and set

 X_i=(x_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)x_i^1),
 G_i=(g_i^0,sqrt(epsilon)g_i^1),
 F_i=f_i^0+epsilon f_i^1,

with the zero optimum sample. GD still uses the same full word.
Every original residual is Q_ik^0+epsilon Q_ik^1. All types except
prefix-to-suffix are sums of nonpositive numbers. For that type
with R_k>0 the sum is at most

       -C0 R_k+epsilon beta R_k=0;

when R_k=0 the auxiliary residual itself is nonpositive. Therefore
all directed comparisons, including the optimum and original terminal
sample, hold. A maximizing i in beta and k=j+1 show that epsilon
is also the largest permitted weight of this fixed auxiliary data
against this fixed base coordinate. No residual was discarded.

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290, proof paragraph2, these planar jets have
an everywhere finite convex differentiable1-smooth interpolant with
minimum value0 at0. Concretely, with I={star,0,...,n}, define

 c_i=<G_i,X_i>-F_i,
 J_i(v)=c_i+<X_i,v-G_i>+||v-G_i||^2/2,
 J(v)=max_(i in I) J_i(v),
 F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-J(v)}.

The cited fact proves F(X_i)=F_i and grad F(X_i)=G_i from exactly
the residual convention just verified. Thus these are actual GD data
of one function, not a sum of separately illegal functions.

If beta=0, apply the same accepted interpolation construction directly
to the auxiliary scalar jets, since their full residual list is legal.
No division by beta occurs in this case.

## 5. Exact energy calculation

For the auxiliary suffix, f_i^1-f_n^1=q^2 R_i and
f_n^1=q^2/(2kappa). The elementary finite identity

 2sum_(i>j) h_i R_i = R^2+sum_(i>j)h_i^2

follows by expanding R^2. Thus the combined suffix contribution of
the weighted value differences and gradient diagonals is R(R+1)q^2.
The prefix contribution is

 kappa sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2-H q^2/kappa.

Subtracting the terminal s(s+1)q^2 gives(1), because
s(s+1)-R(R+1)=H(s+R+1).

The primitive energy is linear in all values and Gram entries.
The repaired planar trajectory therefore has energy

          V0+(C0/beta)V_kappa

when beta>0, and the legal auxiliary trajectory has energy V_kappa
when beta=0. Accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10, says an original
full nonnegative flow/PSD certificate forces nonnegative energy on
every actual trajectory. This proves(2), including beta=0.

## 6. The unit-curvature slice and exact low-degree polynomial interface

At kappa=1, q=p_(j+1)/(1+R)=-eta p_j, t_kappa=0, and

 beta=max{0,max_(i<=j)q(p_i-q)}, V_1=1-A^2 q^2.

For the last identity, the prefix sum in(1) telescopes to
1-p_(j+1)^2, using p_(i+1)^2=(1-h_i)^2 p_i^2. Then
(1+R)^2+H(s+R+2)=A^2. This rederives the unit-curvature
repair without treating the shared max2 draft as a correctness premise.

For R>0 the entire curvature interval can be expressed through
finitely many POLYNOMIAL branches. Define

 d_i(kappa)=[E p_i-p_(j+1)]/kappa,                     0<=i<=j,
 T_i(kappa)=2R p_(j+1)d_i-(1-kappa)d_i^2,
 T_max(kappa)=max{0,T_0(kappa),...,T_j(kappa)},

 P_cut(kappa)=E^2 sum_(i<=j)h_i[1+kappa(1-h_i)]p_i^2
              -H[1+kappa(s+R+1)]p_(j+1)^2.

The numerator defining d_i vanishes at kappa=0, so d_i is a
polynomial, including at0. It has degree at most j. Consequently
T_i has degree at most2j+1, and P_cut degree at most2j+3.
The exact identities are

 beta=kappa^3 T_max/(2R E^2),
 V_kappa=kappa P_cut/E^2.

Since kappa,R,E are positive, (2) is equivalent to

 kappa^2 V0 T_max(kappa)+2R C0 P_cut(kappa)>=0,
                                             0<kappa<=1. (3)

At each branch where0 or a specific T_i is the maximum, (3) is
a polynomial inequality of degree at most2j+3. For a four-step word,
the two middle cuts j=1,2 therefore require degrees at most5,7.
Their branch selection inequalities are the explicit comparisons
T_i>=0 and T_i>=T_k, with the zero branch selected when all T_i<=0.
This gives a precise exact real-algebraic interface for further work;
no polynomial-root search or numerical interval scan is performed.

## 7. Scope and remaining task

The construction gives legal adversaries whenever(2) fails. Passing
all these tests gives no full certificate; no sufficiency is claimed.
The convex combination mechanism retains every original interpolation
pair and all function-value offsets. A favorable or unfavorable outcome
on any finite list of curvatures would not settle the universal interval.

No new word was selected, tested, rounded, or evaluated in deriving this
family. It neither constructs the missing four-step seed nor excludes
every h with sum>=168/25. The accepted conditional lift remains available
as fact04219554e41d9750; the seed existence problem is still OPEN.


# Appendix B: all additional max2 scalar tests, reproduced and audited

# Exact survivor of the complete current scalar necessary-test system

Status: self-contained joint proof DRAFT, not a verifier-accepted fact.
This file establishes a limitation of a specified collection of necessary
conditions. It does NOT supply nonnegative multipliers or a full PSD seed.
No optimizer, numerical parameter search, grid, verifier, or installation
was used. The two tiny exact rational calculations expand the fixed
polynomial identities printed below; they do not search for a schedule.

## 1. Exact schedule and variable-capacity family

For 0<=t<=1/100 define

 h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4),
 a=h0-1=2/5, b=h1-1=5/4, c=h2-1=8/25+t, d=h3-1=3/4,
 C=1+sum h=193/25+t,
 P=2+a=12/5, Q=3+a+b=93/20,
 D=2+d=11/4, H=3+c+d=407/100+t.

All previous max2 scalar tests, and the complete C82 triangle family,
are STRICTLY satisfied throughout 0<=t<=1/100. In addition, every
quadratic-curvature test and every C83 cut-repair test is STRICTLY
satisfied for every 0<k<=1 throughout 0<=t<=10^(-12).

Thus there is an exact survivor at total sum168/25 and a concrete
interval of survivors with strictly larger sums. In particular an
attempt to prove emptiness from these scalar tests cannot succeed.
This is not a restriction or a reduction of the original feasible set
onto a fixed-capacity slice; it is a constructed family inside the
necessary-test region.

The original full interface remains twenty Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j in0..4,
Lambda_ii=0, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,
L^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2sum h)^T, and

 diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)) + (LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0,
 s=sum h.

No Lambda or matrix remainder is claimed in this file. Accepted
04219554e41d9750 gives the conditional asymptotic implication only
if an actual full certificate is subsequently constructed.

## 2. Compact box, every cut, and the elementary nonlinear tests

On our interval, 193/25<=C<=773/100<87/10, 8/25<=c<=33/100,
and all four shifts are positive. The endpoint bounds a,d<77/97 and
middle bounds b,c<1552559/1081453 hold: b=5/4 gives
4*1552559-5*1081453=802971>0, and c< b. Thus the strict step box
from the earlier joint draft is satisfied.

The four cut inequalities, in order, are

 Ca <= C-1-a,
 Cb <= P H,
 Cc <= Q D,
 Cd <= C-1-d.                                           (1)

The first and fourth have margins respectively
(3/5)C-7/5>0 and C/4-7/4>0. The second has margin

 PH-Cb=59/500+(23/20)t>0.                                (2)

For the third, Cc<=25509/10000<64/25, whereas QD=1023/80>12.
These bounds follow by multiplying the upper endpoints of C and c.
Every prefix/reversed-prefix cut recurrence is merely a rearrangement
of (1), so no extra cut has been dropped. The concavity-derived endpoint
sum condition follows from the two middle cuts: if E=4+a+d, adding
b<=P(C-P)/(C+P), c<=D(C-D)/(C+D), and using the negative second
derivative of z(C-z)/(C+z), gives E>=2C(C-1)/(3C+1).
The pointwise endpoint and middle cut bounds follow directly from (1).

The actual unit-curvature product test, suffix product test, and
chronological clipped-tail test are, respectively,

 C a b c d<=1,   C c d<=Q,
 C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P.                              (3)

Here ab=1/2, bc<=33/80<1, and Cc<64/25. Consequently their left
sides are less than

 (3/8)(64/25)=24/25<1,
 (3/4)(64/25)=48/25<Q,
 (15/16)(64/25)=12/5=P.

All comparisons are strict because 25509<25600.
At t=0 the exact full product is579/625 and the exact clipped
left side is579/250, whose gap from P is21/250.

The scalar mixed first-prefix test has u=7/5 and eta=b/H. Its expression
is

 u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-a C^2 eta^2
   =59/25+(7/125)eta-(2/5)(C eta)^2.

By (1), C eta<P=12/5. Therefore this expression exceeds7/125>0.
Its domain a eta<1 also follows since a eta<ab/4=1/8.

For the two unit-curvature cut repairs, the base energy is
V0=(1+prefix sum)^2-C^2 eta^2>0 by (1). The auxiliary terminal
value q is q=1/(2H) at cut1, and q=-c/(2D) at cut2. Hence

 C|q|=C/(2H)<1 at cut1, since 2H-C=C-73/10>0,
 C|q|=Cc/(2D)<(64/25)/(11/2)<1 at cut2.

The auxiliary energy1-C^2 q^2 is strictly positive. Thus every repair
expression beta V0+eta(1+eta)(1-C^2 q^2) is positive for beta>=0,
including the beta=0 case. No denominator with an unproved sign is used.

## 3. All three adjacent triangles and the first backward lift

The adjacent-triangle tests, with the selected consecutive shifts
B,E, preceding capacity P0 and following capacity D0, read

 B E [1+B(P0+C)/P0^2] <= D0.                            (4)

For the first pair, its left side is
ab[1+a(1+C)] < (1/2)[1+(2/5)*9]=23/10<H.
For the middle pair, bc<=33/80, P+C<11, and P^2>5. Therefore its
left side is less than

 (33/80)[1+(5/4)*11/5]=99/64<11/4=D.

For the last pair, cd<=99/400, Q+C<13 and Q^2>21. Its left side
is less than

 (99/400)[1+(33/100)*13/21]
 <(99/400)(5/4)=99/320<1.

The strengthened middle triangle has the additional nonnegative
parameter max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0). In this family

 a^2+2a-1=-1/25,

so this parameter equals zero and the strengthened test is exactly
the already strict middle inequality. This is an actual branch check,
not deletion of an active max term.

## 4. Both last-pair lifts and the earliest-normal guard

Define the last-triangle quantities

 eta=c/D,
 K=c^2 d+(c+d+2)(1-cd)=c+d+2-cd(d+2),
 r=c^2 d/K,
 Hpre=2+a+b=73/20, Apre=1+a+b=53/20,
 Z=(c+r)/(1+d),
 qlate=(1+(1-b)eta)/b,
 qearly=[eta+(Apre eta-1)Z]/Apre,
 Jpre=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 max(qlate,qearly).

The required inequalities are C^2 r<=min(Jpre,Q^2) and

 C^2 max(eta^2,r)<=Jquad,
 Jquad=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).             (5)

All denominators are positive: eta<3/25, cd<1/4, c+d+2>3, so
K>3-(1/4)(11/4)=37/16>2. Also

 r < ((33/100)^2*(3/4))/2 <1/20,
 eta^2<9/625<1/20, C^2<64.

Thus every left side in (5) and C^2 r is less than16/5.
On the right, qlate>(1-3/100)/(5/4)=97/125>3/4,
Hpre>7/2,Hpre<4, and eta<3/25. Therefore

 Jpre >=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 qlate
       >1-24/25+147/16>9.

Also Q^2>21. For Jquad use b eta<3/20, a^2 b=1/5 and1+b=9/4:

 Jquad >[(17/20)*21+5]/(9/4)=457/45>10.

These bounds already retain the full max in Jpre. In fact the earliest
normal is inactive here for a proved reason: Apre eta<(53/20)(3/25)<1.
Since Z>0, the explicit formula gives
qearly<eta/Apre<eta<3/25<3/4<qlate. No shortcut based only on
cd<1 or b eta<1 has been made.
The other max is also correctly determined: d=3/4>sqrt(2)-1, so
r>eta^2. This follows from

d(d+2)^2-K=(c+d+2)(d(d+2)-1)>0.

At t=0, K=241/100,r=192/6025,eta=32/275,
qlate=1068/1375, and Jquad=529303/49500.

## 5. The entire C82 interior-edge triangle family survives

For clarity, the interface is rederived here in this family's notation.
Take U>0,z>0, with

 U>=b,
 z^2 <= (U+1)(a U-1),
 z^2 >= c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2.                              (6)

Use vertices p=(U,0), q=(-1,z), w=(-bc/D,0), and the convex
function F(x)=max_{v in conv(p,q,w)}(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).
Its gradient is projection onto that triangle. The original jets are

 x0=((1+a)U-1,z), g0=(U,0),
 x1=(-1,z), g1=(-1,z),
 x2=(b,-bz), g2=(b,0),
 x3=(-bc,-bz), g3=(-bc/D,0),
 x4=(-bc/D,-bz), g4=(-bc/D,0).

The initial normal comparison with q is z^2-(U+1)(aU-1)<=0;
the other initial comparison is nonpositive since aU-1>=0.
At1 the normal is0. At2 its normal is(0,-bz), and U>=b places
g2 on the base. At3 the comparison with q is
b[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0, and the base comparison is nonpositive.
At4 the normal is again vertical downward. These checks include every
triangle vertex and the optimum0 in its base, hence all original
interpolation pairs. The four GD identities follow by subtraction.
Saturated values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 telescope, giving energy

 E(U,z)=((1+a)U-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2(bc/D)^2.           (7)

The complete parameter-eliminated C82 test is the infimum of (7)
over (6), including zero-height limits. We bound the whole domain,
so no unproved formula for that infimum is needed.
Since a=2/5, (6) forces U>=5/2. Also 1-b^2=-9/16<0, so
E(U,z)>=q(U), where

 q(U)=((7/5)U-1)^2-(9/16)(U+1)((2/5)U-1)-C^2(bc/D)^2.

Its quadratic coefficient is347/200>0 and
q'(5/2)=(7/5)(71/16)>0. Thus q is strictly increasing for U>=5/2.
Using Cc<64/25 and b/D=5/11 gives

 E(U,z)>=q(5/2)=25/4-(5 Cc/11)^2
              >25/4-25/16=75/16>0.

This includes all branches, boundary limits and the exact infimum.
It proves survival of C82, not sufficiency of any scalar test.

# A paid transverse carry and a performance ceiling for the entire retained history

Status: COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max, final allocated
analytical round35. No numerical, solver, machine-algebra, replay,
installation, subagent, external consult, or formal-verifier call is used.
Nothing here is a new fact. The original arbitrary-word asymptotic
exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

The new constructive object is a positive-parallel, genuinely transverse
cell, followed by a later reversal. One static two-dimensional function
retains every old query, pays its actual transverse minimizer displacement,
and returns its full outgoing affine-history/sublevel-distance state.
This is a nonempty two-transition rule on the explicitly stated family.
It is not an all-depth invariant. Its whole parameter family loses to an
accepted alternating-ray witness on the SAME word.

The separate obstruction is stronger than an impossible reversal: on a
specified family of supplied words, EVERY smooth convex extension of the
displayed old jets, in every dimension, with any changed minimum and any
future gradients, has a normalized payoff below the same-word accepted
reference. Thus merely changing the final chronology cannot make this
particular old history competitive there. The adversary may instead choose
its prefix using the whole word; that freedom is not excluded.

Accepted dependencies are fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the original smooth
interpolation inequality, factb98bb807d514e80d for K_n, fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a
for the alternating-ray witness, and fact0be8b54e0a33381c for B_n.
Every other ingredient used is proved inside this draft. Earlier max34
and retained-state findings are context, not premises.

## 1. Normalization, actual jets, and their forced return

Work at smoothness1 in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space. Let e be a
unit vector, and retain the following three actual queried jets, with a
common additive value shift normalized to zero:

    x_A=3e,  g_A=e,   f_A=5/2;
    x_B=2e,  g_B=e,   f_B=3/2;
    x_C=-4e, g_C=-e,  f_C=7/2.                         (1.1)

They form the prefix with steps(1,6), starting at3e. They are legal:
the envelope of J_old(z)=|<e,z>| has scalar value t^2/2 for |t|<=1
and |t|-1/2 for |t|>=1. Its derivative on this line is clip(t,-1,1)e.
Its minimum is0 and its minimizer set is the hyperplane <e,z>=0.
The old proximal data z_i=x_i-g_i and H_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2 are

    (z_A,H_A)=(2e,2), (z_B,H_B)=(e,1),
    (z_C,H_C)=(-3e,3).                                (1.2)

All three heights are strictly positive.

Let F be ANY convex differentiable1-smooth function retaining(1.1),
in any larger finite dimension, with a nonempty minimizer set. The signed
interpolation residual from accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is

    Q_ij=F_i-F_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 <=0.     (1.3)

For 2<=t<=3, convexity between A and B gives F(te)<=t-1/2.
The supporting inequality at B gives the reverse bound. Thus F(te)=t-1/2.
Applying Q_(B,t)<=0 gives ||grad F(te)-e||^2/2<=0, so the FULL gradient
is e, with no transverse component.

The next supplied step13/2 takes C to X=(5/2)e. Any following ordinary
list of nonnegative steps with sum S in[0,1/2] stays on [2e,(5/2)e].
Induction over its partial sums therefore forces every gradient in this
list, including the endpoint gradient, to be e. These conclusions use
actual old jets and smooth convexity; no new plane choice is assumed.

For a parameter delta in[0,1/2], with S+delta<1/2, prescribe

    b=3/2-S-delta >1,     Y=1+delta.

This checkpoint takes the endpoint of that ordinary list to Ye. The
complete word considered in Sections2--4 is

    h=(1,6,13/2, block_S, b, tail),                    (1.4)

where block_S has sum S, and tail is any finite nonnegative list. Write
T=sum(tail). No supplied step is changed to obtain a continuation. The
boundary S+delta=1/2, b=1 also obeys the proofs, but the stated b>1 region
is the interface relevant to the failed old next-block condition. In fact
b<=3/2<T+2 throughout this region, so the former next-mass condition
b>=T+2 fails for every member, including T=0.

## 2. Every changed minimum pays a radius cost

Let mu=min F in the value normalization(1.1), and let x_star be ANY
minimizer. Put t_star=<e,x_star>. In(1.3), the rows from B and C to
the minimizer, and the reverse row from the minimizer to B, give

    Q_(B,star)=t_star-mu <=0,
    Q_(C,star)=-t_star-mu <=0,
    Q_(star,B)=mu-1 <=0.

Consequently

    0<=mu<=1,       |t_star|<=mu.                     (2.1)

In particular, for the TRUE initial distance

    R=dist(3e,argmin F),       R>=3-mu>=2.             (2.2)

These constraints hold for every minimizer, including minimizers outside
the old geometric span. Extra dimensions cannot reduce the e-coordinate
part of this distance. Conversely, no old point is required to remain a
minimizer: (2.1)--(2.2) already permit a completely changed minimum and
minimizer set. This is the normalization used below.

Smoothness at the retained point B gives the value bound

    F(Ye) <= 3/2+(Y-2)+(Y-2)^2/2
           =1+delta^2/2=:C_delta.                     (2.3)

For completeness, the smoothness inequality follows by integrating the
gradient on the segment from B to Ye and using its Lipschitz constant1:
the remainder is at most the integral of t||Ye-B||^2 over0<=t<=1.
No assumption on the gradient at Ye enters(2.3).

## 3. Arbitrary subsequent gradients, with an explicit large-step payment

For any current point x, current gradient g, and h>=0, smoothness gives

    F(x-hg) <= F(x)-h(1-h/2)||g||^2.                  (3.1)

Also F(x)-mu>=||g||^2/2: apply the same inequality with h=1 and use
mu<=F(x-g). Thus each later step has the gap bound

    F(x-hg)-mu <= a(h)[F(x)-mu],
    a(h)=1 if 0<=h<=2, and a(h)=(h-1)^2 if h>2.       (3.2)

Let A_tail be the product of a(h) over the ACTUAL supplied tail, with
empty product1. Iterating(3.2) along its actual GD run gives

    2(F(x_end)-mu)/R^2
       <= A_tail * 2(C_delta-mu)/(3-mu)^2.            (3.3)

The derivative of the last rational factor with respect to mu is

    2(2C_delta-3-mu)/(3-mu)^3 <=0,

because delta<=1/2 and0<=mu<=1. Therefore EVERY such extension satisfies

    V_F:=2(F(x_end)-mu)/R^2
       <= A_tail*(2+delta^2)/9.                      (3.4)

If every tail step lies in[0,2], A_tail=1. There is no constant-gradient,
opposite-gradient, plane-count, fixed-rank, fixed-minimum, or fixed-value
assumption on the new samples. All future values and gradients may change
as allowed by one static F. The dependence on large future steps is the
displayed product, not a suppressed depth-dependent loss.

In particular, a proposed extension that aims at a score M>0 must have

    A_tail >= 9M/(2+delta^2).                         (3.5)

This necessary condition is not sufficient. It does not exclude a
different prefix chosen using the whole word, nor claim that a large-step
suffix must improve the score.

## 4. A uniform same-word performance obstruction

Suppose now that T<=3 and every tail step lies in[0,2]. The step6 at
index1 has preceding sum1 and subsequent sum8-delta+T in(1.4).
Its accepted single-cut score, from factb98bb807d514e80d, is

    K_cut=25/[4(17-2delta+2T)] <= K_n(h).             (4.1)

Combining(3.4) with(4.1),

    V_F/K_cut <=4(17-2delta+2T)(2+delta^2)/225
              <=22/25.                              (4.2)

Here is an exact check of the second inequality. Increasing T to3 leaves
the polynomial (23-2delta)(2+delta^2). Its second derivative is
46-12delta>0 on[0,1/2], so its maximum is at an endpoint. Its endpoint
values are46 and99/2. Multiplication by4/225 gives22/25. Thus the entire
retained history loses at least a factor22/25 to an accepted witness on
the SAME word, even after all possible changes in the future function.

There is a much stronger accepted reference here. In fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a,
select the checkpoints6 and13/2 in exactly this word. Its ordinary masses
are

    s_0=1, s_1=0, s_2=S+b+T=3/2-delta+T.

The accepted recursion gives

    m_0=1/2, O_0=0, m_1=5/2, O_1=1/2,
    z_term=53/4, m_old=1/2, e_term=1/4.

Every required positivity condition holds, and there is no same-ray
magnitude condition before the terminal stage. Since1<=s_2<=9/2,
z_term>=(1+s_2)m_old. Moreover

    q_opt=(53/4)/(4-2delta+2T) >=53/40>1/2,

and q_opt<=z_term/(1+s_2), because2s_2+1>=s_2+1. The actual accepted
alternating-ray construction therefore has payoff

    M_ray=1/2+(2809/16)/(4-2delta+2T)
           >=2889/160.                              (4.3)

This is the payoff of this particular accepted checkpoint selection,
not a claim that the entire F_ray_n(h) maximum was computed. It gives
M_ray<=F_ray_n(h)<=W_n(h). Since(3.4) also gives V_F<=1/4,

    V_F/M_ray <=40/2889.                             (4.4)

This comparison is possible because the competing legal adversary
chooses different early jets on the SAME word. That is precisely the
freedom a whole-word adversary has. Equations(4.2)--(4.4) do not upper-bound
W_n(h), and they are not a minimax disproof or an obstruction to the
original affirmative theorem. These words are already handled strongly
by the accepted scalar construction.

For the previous actual six-step word

    h_A=(1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5,1/20),

delta=1/10 and T=1/20. The full-history bound and references become

    V_F<=67/300,
    K_6(h_A)=125/338,
    M_ray(h_A)=14201/312.                             (4.5)

The complete K maximum is

    max(10/309,125/338,605/4992,40/237699)=125/338.

The indexed terms are the cuts at6,13/2,6/5. The remaining cuts vanish.
The maximum follows from125/338>1/3 and every other term<1/8. The ray
score follows from(4.3) with denominator39/10:
1/2+2809*10/(16*39)=14201/312. These are exact symbolic substitutions,
not numerical evaluations.

The logical obstruction is therefore explicit: a rule that commits to
the old jets(1.1), then promises to recover even the accepted same-word
reference on every member of(1.4) by adjusting subsequent planes,
gradients, or the minimum, is false. The conclusion is conditional on
that particular past commitment; it is not an obstruction to choosing
different old jets after seeing the whole supplied word.

## 5. A genuine transverse escape with one static function

Now construct, rather than forbid, a new chronology. Use R^2 coordinates
(t,s) in the orthonormal directions e,v. Define

    q=(1/2)e+(2/5)v,       r_q=||q||^2=41/100,
    P(z)=1/2+<q,z>=1/2+t/2+2s/5,
    J(z)=max(0,t,-t,P(z),-2P(z)),
    f(x)=min_z [J(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                   (5.1)

This fixes both affine offsets, the old component q_0=e/2, the fresh
component2v/5, and the reversal scale beta=2 TOGETHER. The normals of
the two added planes are q and -2q. Neither cell is optimized separately.
The old positive and negative planes, as well as the zero plane, remain.

Here is a direct justification of the static envelope construction.
The defining minimum exists, since J>=0 and the quadratic is coercive,
and it is unique by strict convexity. If a plane of normal g is active
at z and x=z+g, its supporting inequality shows that z minimizes the
objective: substituting any w gives an excess at least||w-z||^2/2.
Hence f(x)=J(z)+||g||^2/2. For two proximal points z(x),z(y), summing
their subgradient inequalities gives

    ||g(x)-g(y)||^2 <= <g(x)-g(y),x-y>,
    g(x)=x-z(x).

The supporting inequality and evaluation at z(x) bound the value
remainder between0 and||x-y||^2/2, proving differentiability with
gradient g(x). The displayed inequality proves its Lipschitz constant
is at most1. Convexity follows by joint convexity of the proximal
objective. Thus f is an everywhere finite convex1-smooth function.

In proximal coordinates the original residual(1.3) is equivalent to

    H_j >= H_i+<g_i,z_j-z_i>.

Checking every retained or future plane at each proposed proximal point
therefore checks both directions of EVERY original pair. Checking only
adjacent gradients is not the argument being used.

The minimum of J, and hence f, is0. Since J>=|t| and J>=max(P,-2P),
equality requires t=0 and P=0. Its unique minimizer in this plane is

    x_star=-(5/4)v,
    R^2=||3e-x_star||^2=9+25/16=169/16.               (5.2)

The minimum and minimizer set of f equal those of J: both nonnegative
terms in its defining minimum can be zero only at x=z in argmin J.
In higher dimension the unused perpendicular directions are free, and
the nearest minimizer and distance(5.2) are unchanged.

The old origin is NOT a minimizer of this new function. The old minimum
value0 is retained, but its nearest point moves by5/4 in a new direction.
No origin or radius is reset. A contract that also freezes the old
specific minimizer0 would exclude this construction; the legal-history
contract used here retains the queried jets and pays the true distance.

### Every inherited jet, including the remote blocking query, survives

At a positive old proximal coordinate(t,0) with t>=1,

    P(t,0)=(1+t)/2<=t,       -2P(t,0)<=0.

Thus all positive old and returned queries with proximal coordinates
2,1,3/2,13/10 retain their exact plane t. At(-3,0), P=-1 and-2P=2<=3,
so the negative plane also remains active. In particular no earlier
query-height constraint was omitted. The original physical positions
through the return are

    3e, 2e, -4e, (5/2)e, (23/10)e, (11/10)e,

using the exact steps1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5. All their old proximal heights
are positive. At the last point the newly selected gradient is q,
not a negative multiple of e.

### A whole supplied two-block region, with all endpoint comparisons

Append a nonnegative list of sum sigma in[0,1/10], then the prescribed
checkpoint6, then a nonnegative list of sum T in[0,1/20]. This is the
actual word family

    h_D=(1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5, block_sigma,6,block_T).      (5.3)

Its repaired chronology carries a positive e component after the small
checkpoint6/5, then uses the later supplied checkpoint6 for reversal.
Thus it is an escape from the failed reversal timing, not a claim that
the same reversal can be forced at6/5. That small checkpoint violates
the old next-mass cap even when the subsequent first block is empty.

The lists may have any finite length, may be empty when their sum is0,
and may contain zero steps. They are supplied in advance, and(5.1) is
the SAME single static function throughout every such run.

At a partial first-block mass u in[0,sigma], propose gradient q. Its
actual position and proximal point are

    x(u)=Ye-uq, z(u)=Ye-(u+1)q,       Y=11/10.

The selected height and comparisons with the old signed planes are

    P(z(u))=16/25-41u/100,
    P(z(u))-<e,z(u)>=1/25+9u/100>0,
    P(z(u))+<e,z(u)>=31/25-91u/100>0.                 (5.4)

For u<=1/10 the selected height is at least599/1000>0. Hence it also
dominates0 and-2P. These inequalities prove that q is the actual gradient
at every query in this entire first block and at its endpoint.

The checkpoint6 then gives the next start Ye-(sigma+6)q. At partial
second-block mass w in[0,T], propose gradient-2q. Put

    k=sigma+4-2w,    x(w)=Ye-(sigma+6-2w)q,
    z(w)=Ye-kq.

Then39/10<=k<=41/10, and

    <e,z(w)>=11/10-k/2<0,
    P(z(w))=21/20-(41/100)k<0,
    -2P(z(w))-|<e,z(w)>|=(8/25)k-1>=31/125>0.       (5.5)

The selected height is at least549/500>0. It dominates the opposite
old plane, P, and0 as well. This proves the second gradient and every
new/new or old/new plane comparison. The two added planes were checked
at ALL old queries before this calculation, so the future planes cannot
silently overwrite a retained value.

Since every comparison is affine in the partial sums, the same checks
cover all actual intermediate samples of arbitrary nonperiodic lists.
All old and new heights are strictly above the actual minimum0.

The terminal value and normalized payoff are exactly

    f_end=2[(sigma+5-2T)(41/100)-21/20],
    V_D(sigma,T)=16(100+41sigma-82T)/4225.             (5.6)

The perpendicular displacement through the first cell and its checkpoint
is -(2/5)(sigma+6)v. The paid initial transverse distance is5/4; its
squared contribution25/16 is already present in(5.2). These are different
quantities, neither of which has been discarded.

## 6. Exact actual word, full outgoing state, and its normalization

For sigma=1/10 and T=1/20 take one step in each ordinary list:

    h_D=(1,6,13/2,1/5,6/5,1/10,6,1/20).             (6.1)

The positions after Ye=(11/10,0) are

    (21/20,-1/25), (-39/20,-61/25), (-19/10,-12/5).

Their gradients are q,-2q,-2q respectively. The final proximal point is
p_end=(-9/10,-8/5), its height is59/50, and its gradient norm squared
is41/25. Thus

    f_end=59/50+41/50=2,       V_D=64/169.             (6.2)

Here is the complete outgoing retained state for this actual word.
Let u_out=q/||q||=(5e+4v)/sqrt(41), and G_out=2||q||=sqrt(41)/5.
Then x_end=p_end-G_out*u_out, exactly as required for the negative
outgoing boundary gradient. Write ell for its real slice coordinate.
The full retained envelope profile is

    Phi_out(ell)=max(
        |-9/10+5ell/sqrt(41)|,
        -59/100+sqrt(41)ell/10,
         59/50-sqrt(41)ell/5).                        (6.3)

Define the query profile, for each real a_test, by

    Psi_out(a_test)=min_(z,H in D)
                     [H-a_test*<u_out,z-p_end>],      (6.4)

where D contains ALL these actual proximal samples and heights:

    ((2,0),2), ((1,0),1), ((-3,0),3),
    ((3/2,0),3/2), ((13/10,0),13/10),
    ((3/5,-2/5),16/25),
    ((11/20,-11/25),599/1000),
    ((-19/20,-41/25),631/500),
    ((-9/10,-8/5),59/50).

No old query is deleted; coincident endpoints for zero steps can be
retained repeatedly without changing the minimum. For longer lists in
the same blocks, their intermediate contributions are affine between
the corresponding endpoint contributions, so endpoint compression within
each such block is an exact equality for Psi, not an assumption about
unrelated remote queries.

For the full sublevel-distance state, put

    P_ell=-59/100+sqrt(41)ell/10,
    A_ell=P_ell-1/2,
    t_free=(48+50A_ell)/41.

For r_level>=0 with -r_level/2<=P_ell<=r_level, set

    t_clip=min(r_level,max(-r_level,t_free)),
    Xi_out(ell,r_level)=(t_clip-3)^2
                         +(25/4)(A_ell-t_clip/2)^2.   (6.5)

For every other pair(ell,r_level), set Xi_out=+infinity. This is exactly
the squared distance from the ORIGINAL initial point(3,0) to the slice
of {J<=r_level} at <u_out,z-p_end>=ell.

Indeed the slice fixes q.z=A_ell and hence P(z)=P_ell. The sublevel
conditions are |t|<=r_level and -r_level/2<=P_ell<=r_level. Eliminating
s gives s=(5/2)(A_ell-t/2), so the distance squared is the quadratic
displayed in(6.5). Its derivative vanishes at t_free, and its positive
second derivative is41/8. Clipping therefore gives its exact constrained
minimum. At level0 the only feasible slice has

    ell=59/[10sqrt(41)], P_ell=0, A_ell=-1/2,

and(6.5) gives9+25/16=169/16. Thus the actual minimizer and normalization
are recovered from the retained sublevel state itself.

These formulas give a concrete nonempty local rule and exact output.
They do NOT assert that a subsequent arbitrary block will have the same
state shape or useful payoff. No repeated-depth loss estimate or all-word
invariant is implicit in them.

## 7. Compare both actual continuations to the accepted SAME-word references

On the actual eight-step word(6.1), the complete single-cut reference is

    K_8(h_D)=max(10/431,125/582,605/20608,
                40/2873997,125/1408)=125/582.          (7.1)

The indexed terms correspond to steps6,13/2,6/5,6. Each other cut has
zero numerator. The claimed maximum follows from125/582>1/5 and all
other terms<1/10. Our transverse witness exceeds this K score:

    64/169 > 125/582,

because64*582=37248>21125=125*169. This alone is NOT sufficient
performance progress: there is a stronger accepted reference available.

In fact1fca3e5dd555cb4a select the three checkpoints6,13/2,6 in(5.3).
Then s_0=1, s_1=0, s_2=7/5+sigma, s_3=T, and the accepted state is

    m_0=1/2, m_1=5/2, O_1=1/2,
    m_2=265/(48+20sigma)>=53/10,
    O_2=1/2, z_term=5m_2-1/2>=26,
    m_old=5/2, e_term=0.

The same-ray condition m_2>=m_0 holds. For T<=1/20,
z_term>=(1+T)m_old, and q_opt=z_term/(1+2T)>=260/11>5/2.
Thus every word in this whole two-block family has the accepted score

    M_ray_D=z_term^2/(1+2T) >=6760/11.                (7.2)

Equality holds for the exact word(6.1). Meanwhile(5.6) gives
V_D(sigma,T)<=8328/21125<2/5. Hence the ENTIRE explicit transverse
family satisfies

    V_D/M_ray_D <11/16900.                            (7.3)

No maximum over all checkpoint sets is asserted or needed. This one
accepted selection already strictly dominates the whole new family.

For an escape on the UNCHANGED six-step word h_A in(4.5), use the SAME
static function(5.1) and stop after the q-cell with mass1/20; do not
insert the later step6. Equations(5.4) prove every plane comparison.
The terminal position is(43/40,-1/50), its proximal point is
(23/40,-21/50), its height is1239/2000, and

    f_end=1649/2000,
    V_A_tilt=3298/21125.                              (7.4)

Every queried height remains positive and the true radius is still
sqrt(169)/4. This is a genuine nonreversing/transverse continuation of
the formerly trapped history on the ORIGINAL supplied word. It is better
than the old static Huber continuation11/90, since
3298*90=296820>232375=11*21125. Nevertheless

    3298/21125 <67/300 <125/338 <14201/312.             (7.5)

The first inequality is3298*300=989400<1415375=67*21125;
the second follows by cross multiplication, and the last is immediate
from14201/312>1 and125/338<1. Thus the actual escape, the full-history
upper bound, the single-cut score, and the stronger accepted ray score
are all ordered on the SAME word. The eight-step family is a separate
explicit family, not a repair obtained by silently changing h_A.

## 8. Comparison to the COMPLETE exact B_n benchmark

The accepted B_n recursion includes the split

    B_n>=G(1,B_(n-1)),
    G(1,D)=2+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8D)/2, B_0=1.

For D>=1, sqrt(D^2+8D)>=D+2, since the difference of the squares is
4(D-1)>=0. Induction gives B_n>=3n+1. This is a proved bound on the
ORIGINAL all-split recurrence; no numerical DP or dyadic substitute is
